[^144-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Additional distraint

#### Art. 145

[^145-1]

<sup>1</sup> If the proceeds do not cover the amount of the claims, the debt enforcement office shall immediately execute additional distraint proceedings and realise the assets as quickly as possible. A special request by a creditor is not necessary and the office is not bound by the ordinary time limits.

<sup>2</sup> If further distraint proceedings have been carried out in the meantime, the rights arising from them shall not be affected by the subsequent distraint proceedings.

<sup>3</sup> The provisions on participating in distraint proceedings (Arts 110 and 111) apply.

[^145-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3. Schedule of claims and distribution list

### a. Ranking of creditors

#### Art. 146

[^146-1]

<sup>1</sup> If not all creditors can be satisfied, the debt enforcement office shall draw up the plan for the ranking of creditors (schedule of claims) and the distribution list.

<sup>2</sup> The creditors receive the ranking that they would have in the debtor’s bankruptcy in accordance with Article 219. Instead of the date of the declaration of bankruptcy, the date of the request for continuation is decisive.

[^146-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### b. Availability for inspection

#### Art. 147

[^147-1]

The schedule of claims and the distribution list shall be made available for public inspection at the debt enforcement office. The office shall notify the parties of this and issue each creditor with an extract in respect of his claim.

[^147-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### c. Contesting by legal action

#### Art. 148

<sup>1</sup> If a creditor wishes to contest the claim or the rank of another creditor, he must file an action to contest the schedule of claims against that other creditor within 20 days of receiving the extract in the court at the place of debt enforcement.[^148-1]

<sup>2</sup> …[^148-2]

<sup>3</sup> If the court upholds the action, it shall assign to the plaintiff the share of the proceeds of realisation allocated to the defendant in the distribution list that is required to cover the plaintiff’s loss shown in the distribution list and the legal costs. Any surplus remains with the defendant.[^148-3]

[^148-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^148-2]: Repealed by Annex 1 No II 17 of the Civil Procedure Code of 19 Dec. 2008, with effect from 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 1739](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/262); [BBl 2006 7221](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/914)).
[^148-3]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 4. Unpaid debt certificate

### a. Issue and effect

#### Art. 149

<sup>1</sup> Each creditor that has participated in the distraint proceedings shall receive an unpaid debt certificate for the unpaid amount of his claim. The debtor shall be issued with a copy of the unpaid debt certificate.[^149-1]

<sup>1bis</sup> The debt enforcement office shall issue the unpaid debt certificate as soon as the amount of the shortfall is established.[^149-2]

<sup>2</sup> The unpaid debt certificate constitutes an acknowledgement of the debt in terms of Article 82 and gives the creditor the rights mentioned in Article 271 number 5 and Article 285.

<sup>3</sup> The creditor may continue the debt enforcement proceedings for six months after receiving the unpaid debt certificate without obtaining a new summons for payment.

<sup>4</sup> The debtor is not required to pay interest on the claim documented by the unpaid debt certificate. Joint debtors, guarantors and other persons entitled to take recourse who are required to pay interest in the debtor’s stead may not require him to reimburse the same.

<sup>5</sup> …[^149-3]

[^149-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^149-2]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^149-3]: Repealed by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### b. Statute of limitations and deletion

#### Art. 149a

[^149a-1]

<sup>1</sup> The claim documented by the unpaid debt certificate becomes time barred 20 years after the issue of the unpaid debt certificate; in relation to the debtor’s heirs, however, the claim becomes time barred one year at the latest after the process of succession begins.

<sup>2</sup> The debtor may settle the claim at any time by making payment at the debt enforcement office that issued the unpaid debt certificate. The office shall forward the paid amount to the creditor or if applicable pay it into the official depositary.

<sup>3</sup> Following settlement, the entry in respect of the unpaid debt certificate shall be deleted from the registers. Written confirmation shall be sent to the debtor on request.

[^149a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 5. Handing over the document acknowledging the debt

#### Art. 150

<sup>1</sup> If a creditor's claim is fully satisfied, the creditor must endorse the document acknowledging the debt and hand it over to the debt enforcement officer to be passed on to the debtor.[^150-1]

<sup>2</sup> If a claim is only partially satisfied, the creditor retains the document; the debt enforcement office must certify on the same or have the competent authority certify the amount of debt still due.

<sup>3</sup> In the case of the realisation of immovable property, the debt enforcement office shall arrange for the necessary deletions and amendments to easements, real burdens, mortgages and registered personal rights in the land register.[^150-2]

[^150-1]: Amended by Art. 58 Final Title CC, in force since 1 Jan. 1912 ([AS 24 233](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/24/233_245_233)Art. 60 Final Title CC; [BBl 1904 IV 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1904/4_1_1_), [1907 VI 367](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1907/6_367__)).
[^150-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

## Title Four Debt Enforcement by Realising a Pledge

### A. Request for debt enforcement

#### Art. 151

[^151-1]

<sup>1</sup>

Any person who initiates debt enforcement proceedings for a claim secured by a pledge (Art. 37) must, in addition to the details listed in Article 67, specify the pledged asset in the request for debt enforcement. In addition, the following must be stated in the request where applicable:

- a. the name of the third party who created the pledge or acquired ownership of the pledged asset;
- b.[^151-2] the use of pledged immovable property as a family home (Art. 169 CC[^151-3]) or as a joint home (Art. 14 Same-Sex Partnership Act of 18 June 2004[^151-4]) for the debtor or the third party.

<sup>2</sup> If a creditor initiates debt enforcement proceedings on the basis of a pledge on which a third party has a subordinate pledge (Art. 886 CC), the creditor must notify the third party of the initiation of the debt enforcement proceedings.

[^151-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^151-2]: Amended by Annex No 16 of the Same-Sex Partnership Act of 18 June 2004, in force since 1 Jan. 2007 ([AS 2005 5685](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2005/782); [BBl 2003 1288](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2003/194)).
[^151-3]: [SR 210](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5703)
[^151-4]: [SR 211.231](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5750)

### B. Summons for payment

### 1. Content. Notice to tenants and lessees

#### Art. 152

<sup>1</sup>

On receipt of the request for debt enforcement, the debt enforcement office shall issue a summons for payment in accordance with Article 69, but with the following special features:[^152-1]

- The payment period to be set for the debtor is one month in the case of a lien on property, six months in the case of a mortgage.
- The warning states that if the debtor neither complies with nor files an objection to the summons for payment, the pledge will be realised.

<sup>2</sup> If immovable property is subject to a lease or usufructuary lease and the enforcing pledgee requests that liability under the pledge be extended to include the rent claims (Art. 806 CC[^152-2]), the debt enforcement office shall notify the tenants or lessees of the initiation of debt enforcement proceedings and instruct them to pay the rent that is due to the debt enforcement office.[^152-3]

[^152-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^152-2]: [SR 210](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5703)
[^152-3]: Inserted by Art. 58 Final Title CC ([AS 24 233](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/24/233_245_233)Art. 60 Final Title CC; [BBl 1904 IV 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1904/4_1_1_), [1907 VI 367](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1907/6_367__)). Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Official copies. Status of a third-party owner of the pledged asset

#### Art. 153

<sup>1</sup> The summons for payment shall be issued in accordance with Article 70.

<sup>2</sup> The third party and the spouse may file an objection to the summons in the same way as the debtor.[^153-1]

The debt enforcement office shall also serve a summons for payment on the following persons:

- a. a third party who has created the pledge or acquired ownership of the pledged asset;
- b.[^153-2] the spouse, the registered partner of the debtor or the third party if pledged immovable property serves as a family home (Art. 169 CC[^153-3]) or as a joint home (Art. 14 Same-Sex Partnership Act of 18 June 2004[^153-4]).

<sup>2bis</sup> The persons referred to in paragraph 2 may file an objection to the summons in the same way as the debtor.[^153-5]

<sup>3</sup> If the third party has initiated redemption proceedings (Arts 828 and 829 CC), immovable property may only be realised if the creditor instituting the proceedings proves to the debt enforcement office that he is still the creditor to a mortgage on the property for the claim being enforced.[^153-6]

<sup>4</sup> In all other respects, the provisions of Articles 71–86 apply with regard to the summons for payment and objection to the summons.[^153-7]

[^153-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^153-2]: Amended by Annex No 16 of the Same-Sex Partnership Act of 18 June 2004, in force since 1 Jan. 2007 ([AS 2005 5685](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2005/782); [BBl 2003 1288](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2003/194)).
[^153-3]: [SR 210](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5703)
[^153-4]: [SR 211.231](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5750)
[^153-5]: Inserted by Annex No 16 of the Same-Sex Partnership Act of 18 June 2004, in force since 1 Jan. 2007 ([AS 2005 5685](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2005/782); [BBl 2003 1288](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2003/194)).
[^153-6]: Inserted by Art. 58 Final Title CC ([AS 24 233](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/24/233_245_233)Art. 60 Final Title CC; [BBl 1904 IV 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1904/4_1_1_), [1907 VI 367](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1907/6_367__)). Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^153-7]: Originally para. 3.

### C. Objection to the summons. Cancellation of the notice to tenants and lessees

#### Art. 153a

[^153a-1]

<sup>1</sup> If an objection to the summons for payment is filed, the creditor may, within ten days of receiving notice of the objection, demand that the objection be set aside or file an action for recognition of the claim or for establishment of the pledge.

<sup>2</sup> If the creditor is unsuccessful in having the objection set aside, he may file an action within ten days of receiving notice of the decision[^153a-2].

<sup>3</sup> If he does not comply with these deadlines, notice to tenants and lessees shall be cancelled.

[^153a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^153a-2]: Term pursuant to Annex 1 no II 17 of the Civil Procedure Code of 19 Dec. 2008, in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 1739](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/262); [BBl 2006 7221](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/914)). This amendment has been made throughout the text.

### D. Deadlines for realisation

#### Art. 154

<sup>1</sup> The creditor may request the realisation of a pledged chattel no earlier than one month and no later than one year, and the realisation of a mortgage no earlier than six months and no later than two years after service of the summons for payment. If an objection to the summons is filed, these time limits shall be suspended between the initiation and conclusion of any court proceedings brought as a result.[^154-1]

<sup>2</sup> If the application for realisation is not filed or withdrawn and not renewed within the statutory period, the debt enforcement shall lapse.

[^154-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### E. Realisation procedure

### 1. Initiation

#### Art. 155

<sup>1</sup> If the creditor has made the request for realisation, Articles 97 paragraph 1, 102 paragraph 3, 103 and 106–109 apply to the pledge *mutatis mutandis*.[^155-1]

<sup>2</sup> The debt enforcement office shall notify the debtor of the request for realisation within three days.

[^155-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Procedure

#### Art. 156

[^156-1]

<sup>1</sup> Articles 122–143*b* apply to realisation*.* However, the auction terms (Art. 135) shall stipulate that the share of the sale price due to the pledgee be paid in cash unless the parties involved agree otherwise. They shall also stipulate that the encumbrance on the property that existed in favour of the debtor be deleted from the land register.

<sup>2</sup> In the event of separate realisation, pledge instruments in the name of the owner or in bearer form and pledged by the landowner are reduced to the amount of the proceeds of realisation.

[^156-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3. Distribution

#### Art. 157

<sup>1</sup> The costs of administration, realisation and distribution are paid in advance from the pledge proceeds.[^157-1]

<sup>2</sup> The net proceeds shall be paid to the pledgees up to the amount of their claims, including interest up to the time of the last realisation and the costs of enforcement.[^157-2]

<sup>3</sup> If not all the pledgees can be satisfied, the debt enforcement officer shall determine the ranking of the creditors and their shares, taking account of Article 219 paragraphs 2 and 3.

<sup>4</sup> Articles 147, 148 and 150 apply accordingly.

[^157-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^157-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 4. Pledge shortfall certificate

#### Art. 158

<sup>1</sup> If the pledge could not be realised because the bids were insufficient (Arts 126 and 127) or if the proceeds do not cover the claim, the debt enforcement office shall issue the enforcing pledgee with a pledge shortfall certificate.[^158-1]

<sup>2</sup> After service of this document, the creditor may pursue debt enforcement by way of distraint or bankruptcy, depending on the person of the debtor, unless the debt is a land charge certificate (Art. 33*a* Final Title CC[^158-2]) or another real burden. If the debtor brings debt enforcement proceedings within one month, a new summons for payment is not required.[^158-3]

<sup>3</sup> The pledge shortfall certificate is deemed to be an acknowledgement of debt within the meaning of Article 82.[^158-4]

[^158-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^158-2]: [SR 210](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5703)
[^158-3]: Amended by no II 4 of the FA of 11 Dec. 2009 (Register Mortgage Certificates and Other Amendments to Property Law), in force since 1 Jan. 2012 ([AS 2011 4637](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2011/666); [BBl 2007 5283](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2007/756)).
[^158-4]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

## Title Five Enforcement through Bankruptcy

### I. Ordinary Debt Enforcement through Bankruptcy

### A. Notice of impending bankruptcy

### 1. Timing

#### Art. 159

[^159-1]

If the debtor is subject to debt enforcement through bankruptcy, the debt enforcement office shall give notice of impending bankruptcy immediately on receipt of the request for continuation.

[^159-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Content

#### Art. 160

<sup>1</sup>

The notice of impending bankruptcy contains:

- 1\. the details of the request for debt enforcement;
- 2\. the date of the summons for payment;
- 3\.[^160-1] notice that the creditor may file a petition for bankruptcy after 20 days;
- 4\.[^160-2] notice that the debtor who wishes to contest the admissibility of debt enforcement through bankruptcy must appeal to the supervisory authority within ten days (Art. 17).

<sup>2</sup> The debtor is also reminded that he is entitled to propose a composition agreement.

[^160-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^160-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3. Service

#### Art. 161

<sup>1</sup> Article 72 applies to the service of the notice of impending bankruptcy.[^161-1]

<sup>2</sup> A copy thereof shall be served on the creditor as soon as service has been effected on the debtor.

<sup>3</sup> …[^161-2]

[^161-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^161-2]: Repealed by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, with effect from 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### B. Inventory of assets

### 1. Order

#### Art. 162

The court responsible for opening bankruptcy proceedings (bankruptcy court) shall, at the request of the creditor, order an inventory of all the debtor’s assets (inventory of assets) to be recorded if this appears necessary for the creditor's security.

### 2. Execution

#### Art. 163

<sup>1</sup> The debt enforcement office shall record the inventory of assets. It may not begin until the notice of impending bankruptcy has been served, except in the cases referred to in Articles 83 paragraph 1 and 183.[^163-1]

<sup>2</sup> Articles 90-92 apply accordingly.

[^163-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3. Effects

### a. Obligations of the debtor

#### Art. 164

[^164-1]

<sup>1</sup> The debtor is obliged, subject to criminal penalties for failing to do so (Art. 169 SCC[^164-2]), to ensure that the assets recorded are preserved or replaced by equivalent assets; however, he may use or consume as many of the assets as, at the discretion of the debt enforcement officer, are necessary to support himself and his family.

<sup>2</sup> The debt enforcement officer shall expressly draw the debtor’s attention to his obligations and to the criminal penalties for failing to abide by them.

[^164-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^164-2]: [SR 311.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6196)

### b. Duration

#### Art. 165

<sup>1</sup> The debt enforcement officer shall release the debtor from the duties imposed by the inventory of assets if all the creditors in the enforcement proceedings agree.

<sup>2</sup> The duties expire by law four months after the recording of the inventory.[^165-1]

[^165-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### C. Bankruptcy petition

### 1. Deadline

#### Art. 166

<sup>1</sup> Once 20 days have elapsed since service of the notice of impending bankruptcy, the creditor may file a petition for bankruptcy with the bankruptcy court by submitting the notice of impending bankruptcy and the summons for payment.

<sup>2</sup> This right expires 15 months after service of the summons for payment. If an objection to the summons has been filed, this period shall be suspended between the initiation and conclusion of any legal proceedings brought as a result.[^166-1]

[^166-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Withdrawal

#### Art. 167

If the creditor withdraws the bankruptcy petition, he may not file it again before one month has lapsed.

### 3. Bankruptcy hearing

#### Art. 168

If the bankruptcy petition has been filed, the parties shall be given notice of a court hearing at least three days in advance. They shall be free to appear in court, either in person or by proxy.

### 4. Liability for the bankruptcy costs

#### Art. 169

<sup>1</sup> The person who files the bankruptcy petition is liable for the costs incurred up to and including the discontinuation of bankruptcy proceedings due to a lack of assets (Art. 230) or until the call to creditors (Art. 232).[^169-1]

<sup>2</sup> The court may demand an advance of the costs from the creditor.

[^169-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 5. Interim orders

#### Art. 170

Immediately after the petition for bankruptcy has been filed, the court may issue interim orders necessary to safeguard the rights of the creditors.

### D. Decision of the bankruptcy court

### 1. Declaration of bankruptcy

#### Art. 171

[^171-1]

The court shall issue its decision without delay, even in the absence of the parties. It shall declare bankruptcy unless one of the cases mentioned in Articles 172-173*a* applies.

[^171-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Dismissal of the bankruptcy petition

#### Art. 172

The court shall dismiss the bankruptcy petition:

- 1\. if the notice of impending bankruptcy has been revoked by the supervisory authority;
- 2\.[^172-1] if the debtor has been granted the reinstatement of a deadline (Art. 33 para. 4) or a subsequent objection to the summons for payment has been allowed (Art. 77);
- 3\. if the debtor proves by documentary evidence that the debt, including interest and costs, has been repaid or that the creditor has granted the debtor a deferral.

[^172-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3. Deferral of the decision

### a. Due to the suspension of debt enforcement proceedings or grounds for nullity

#### Art. 173

<sup>1</sup> If the suspension of debt enforcement proceedings is ordered by the supervisory authority following an objection or by the court in accordance with Article 85 or 85*a* paragraph 2, the court shall defer the decision on bankruptcy.[^173-1]

<sup>2</sup> If the court finds on its own initiative that an order (Art. 22 para. 1) issued in the previous proceedings was null and void, it shall also defer the decision and refer the case to the supervisory authority.[^173-2]

<sup>3</sup> The bankruptcy court shall be notified of the supervisory authority's decision and issue its decision thereafter.

[^173-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^173-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### b. Due to the application for a composition moratorium or emergency moratorium or *ex officio*

#### Art. 173a

[^173a-1]

<sup>1</sup> If the debtor or a creditor has applied for a composition moratorium or emergency moratorium, the court may defer the decision on bankruptcy.[^173a-2]

<sup>2</sup> The court may also defer the decision on bankruptcy *ex officio* if direct debt restructuring or the conclusion of a composition agreement appears to be possible; it shall transfer the files to the composition court.[^173a-3]

<sup>3</sup> …[^173a-4]

[^173a-1]: Inserted by Art. 12 of the FA of 28 Sept. 1949 (AS **1950** I 57; [BBl 1948 I 1218](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1948/1_1218_1201_)). Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^173a-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).
[^173a-3]: Amended by Annex No 4 of the FA of 19 June 2020 (Company Law), in force since 1 Jan. 2023 ([AS 2020 4005](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/746); [2022 109](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/109); [BBl 2017 399](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2017/112)).
[^173a-4]: Repealed by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, with effect from 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 3<sup>bis</sup>. Jurisdiction of the Swiss Financial Market Supervisory Authority

#### Art. 173b

[^173b-1]

<sup>1</sup> If the bankruptcy petition relates to a debtor that is subject to the bankruptcy jurisdiction of the Swiss Financial Market Supervisory Authority (FINMA) under the financial market legislation in accordance with Article 1 of the Financial Market Supervision Act of 22 June 2007[^173b-2], the bankruptcy court shall refer the files to FINMA. The latter shall proceed in accordance with the special rules.

<sup>2</sup> Only debtors who have the required FINMA licence are subject to FINMA's bankruptcy jurisdiction.[^173b-3]

[^173b-1]: Inserted by No II 1 of the FA of 3 Oct. 2003 ([AS 2004 2767](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2004/340); [BBl 2002 8060](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2002/1290)). Amended by Annex No II 5 of the Financial Institutions Act of 15 June 2018, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2018 5247](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2018/801), [2019 4631](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/762); [BBl 2015 8901](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2015/2141)).
[^173b-2]: [SR 956.1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/11614)
[^173b-3]: Inserted by Annex No 3 of the FA of 17 Dec. 2021 (Insolvency and Contribution Security), in force since 1 Jan. 2023 ([AS 2022 732](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/732); [BBl 2020 6359](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2020/1555)).

### 4. Appellate remedies

#### Art. 174

[^174-1]

<sup>1</sup> The decision of the bankruptcy court may be contested within ten days by means of an appeal in accordance with the CPC[^174-2]. The parties may assert new facts if these occurred before the decision of the court of first instance.

<sup>2</sup>

The appellate court may revoke the declaration of bankruptcy if the debtor provides credible evidence of his solvency and proves by documentary evidence that in the meantime:

- 1\. the debt, including interest and costs, has been repaid;
- 2\. the amount owed has been deposited with the superior court for transfer to the creditor; or
- 3\. the creditor no longer wishes to continue with the process of debt enforcement through bankruptcy.

<sup>3</sup> If it grants the appeal suspensive effect, it shall at the same time take the necessary interim measures to protect the creditors.

[^174-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).
[^174-2]: [SR 272](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6136)

### E. Time of declaration of bankruptcy

#### Art. 175

<sup>1</sup> Bankruptcy is declared at the time that the ruling is issued.

<sup>2</sup> The court shall state this point in time in the declaration of bankruptcy.

### F. Notice of the court decisions

#### Art. 176

[^176-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The court shall notify the debt enforcement office, the bankruptcy office, the commercial register office and the land register office without delay of:

- 1\. the declaration of bankruptcy;
- 2\. the revocation of the bankruptcy;
- 3\. the conclusion of the bankruptcy proceedings;
- 4\. orders in which it grants suspensive effect to an appeal;
- 5\. interim orders.

<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy must be recorded in the land register no later than two days after it is declared.[^176-2]

[^176-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^176-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 19 March 2004 (Recording of Bankruptcy in the Land Register), in force since 1 Jan. 2005 ([AS 2004 4033](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2004/589); [BBl 2003 6501](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2003/1062)[6509](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2003/1064)).

### II. Debt Enforcement Proceedings Based on a Bill of Exchange

### A. Requirements

#### Art. 177

<sup>1</sup> In the case of claims based on a bill of exchange or cheque, even if they are secured by a pledge, debt enforcement proceedings may be requested at the debt enforcement office if the debtor is subject to debt enforcement through bankruptcy.

<sup>2</sup> The bill of exchange or cheque must be handed over to the debt enforcement office.

### B. Summons for payment

#### Art. 178

<sup>1</sup> If the requirements for debt enforcement proceedings based on a bill of exchange are met, the debt enforcement office shall immediately serve a summons for payment on the debtor.

<sup>2</sup>

The summons for payment shall contain:

- 1\. the details of the request for debt enforcement;
- 2\.[^178-1] the request to satisfy the creditor within five days by settling the claim including debt enforcement costs;
- 3\.[^178-2] notice that the debtor may file an objection to the summons (Art. 179) or file an appeal with the supervisory authority for failure to comply with the law (Arts 17 and 20);
- 4\.[^178-3] notice that the creditor may file a petition for bankruptcy if the debtor fails to comply with the summons even though he has not filed an objection to the summons or his objection to the summons has been set aside (Art. 188).

<sup>3</sup> Articles 70 and 72 apply.

[^178-1]: Amended by Art. 15 para. 4 Final and Transitional Provisions to Titles XXIV-XXXIII CO, in force since 1 July 1937([AS 53 185](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/53/185_185_189); [BBl 1928 I 205](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1928/1_205_233_253), [1932 I 217](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1932/1_217__)).
[^178-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^178-3]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### C. Objection to the summons

### 1. Deadline and form

#### Art. 179

[^179-1]

<sup>1</sup> The debtor may file a written objection with the debt enforcement office within five days of service of the summons for payment; when doing so, he must demonstrate that any one of the conditions set out in Article 182 is met. On request, the debt enforcement office shall confirm the filing of an objection to the summons free of charge.

<sup>2</sup> By stating the grounds for objecting to the summons, the debtor does not waive further defences under Article 182.

<sup>3</sup> Article 33 paragraph 4 does not apply.

[^179-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Notice to the creditor

#### Art. 180

<sup>1</sup> The enforcing creditor shall be informed of the grounds for the objection to the summons for payment on the copy of the summons given to him; if no objection to the summons has been filed, this is noted on the same.

<sup>2</sup> This copy shall be served on the debtor immediately after the objection to the summons has been filed or, if no objection has been filed, immediately after the expiry of the deadline for filing the same.

### 3. Submission to the court

#### Art. 181

[^181-1]

The debt enforcement office shall immediately submit the objection to the summons for payment to the court at the place of debt enforcement. The court shall summon the parties and issue a decision, even in their absence, within ten days of receipt of the objection to the summons.

[^181-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 4. Approval

#### Art. 182

The court shall approve the objection to the summons for payment:

- 1\. if it is proven by documentary evidence that the debt has been paid to the holder of the bill of exchange or cheque or that it has been discharged or deferred by him;
- 2\. if it is credibly asserted that the bill was forged;
- 3\. if a defence arising from the bill of exchange rights appears justified;
- 4\.[^182-1] if another defence admissible under Article 1007 CO[^182-2] is credibly asserted; in this case, however, the amount claimed must be deposited in cash or securities, or equivalent security must be provided.

[^182-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^182-2]: [SR 220](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5818)

### 5. Refusal. Interim measures

#### Art. 183

<sup>1</sup> If the court refuses to approve the objection to the summons for payment, it may take interim measures, and in particular order the recording of an inventory of assets in accordance with Articles 162-165.

<sup>2</sup> If necessary, the court may also require the creditor to provide security.[^183-1]

[^183-1]: Amended by Art. 15 para. 6 Final and Transitional Provisions to Titles XXIV-XXXIII CO, in force since 1 July 1937([AS 53 185](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/53/185_185_189); [BBl 1928 I 205](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1928/1_205_233_253), [1932 I 217](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1932/1_217__)).

### 6. Notice of the decision. Time limit for filing an action in the case of a deposit

#### Art. 184

<sup>1</sup> Notice of the decision on approving the objection to the summons for payment shall be given to the parties immediately.[^184-1]

<sup>2</sup> If the objection to the summons has only been approved after the amount in dispute has been deposited, the creditor shall be requested to raise a claim for payment within ten days. If the creditor does not comply with this request, the deposit shall be returned.

[^184-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 7. Appellate remedies

#### Art. 185

[^185-1]

The decision on the approval of objection to the summons for payment may be contested within five days by an appeal under the CPC[^185-2].

[^185-1]: Amended by Annex 1 No II 17 of the Civil Procedure Code of 19 Dec. 2008, in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 1739](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/262); [BBl 2006 7221](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/914)).
[^185-2]: [SR 272](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6136)

### 8. Effects of the approved objection

#### Art. 186

If the objection is approved, the debt enforcement proceedings shall be discontinued; the creditor must pursue his claim through ordinary civil proceedings.

### D. Action for recovery

#### Art. 187

Any person who has paid an undue debt as a result of failing to file an objection to the summons for payment or the objection not being approved may exercise his right to recover the payment in accordance with Article 86.

### E. Bankruptcy petition

#### Art. 188

<sup>1</sup> If no objection to the summons for payment is filed or if the objection has been set aside but the summons has nevertheless not been complied with, the creditor may file a petition for bankruptcy by submitting the unpaid debt certificate and the summons and, if applicable, the court decision.

<sup>2</sup> This right expires one month after service of the summons. If the debtor has filed an objection to the summons, the time between filing the objection and the corresponding court decision and, if the objection is approved, the time between the raising of the action and the court’s decision in this matter shall not be taken into account.

### F. Decision of the bankruptcy court

#### Art. 189

[^189-1]

<sup>1</sup> The court shall notify the parties of the place, day and hour of the hearing on the bankruptcy petition. It shall issue its decision, even in the absence of the parties, within ten days of the petition being filed.

<sup>2</sup> Articles 169, 170, 172 number 3, 173, 173*a*, 175 and 176 apply.

[^189-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### III. Declaration of Bankruptcy without Prior Debt Enforcement

### A. At the request of a creditor

#### Art. 190

<sup>1</sup>

A creditor may request the court to declare bankruptcy without prior debt enforcement proceedings:

- 1\. against any debtor whose whereabouts are unknown or who has fled in order to evade his debts, or who has committed or attempted to commit fraudulent acts to the detriment of creditors, or who has concealed any of his assets in the course of debt enforcement proceedings by distraint;
- 2\. against a debtor subject to debt enforcement through bankruptcy who has stopped making payments;
- 3\.[^190-1] …

<sup>2</sup> If the debtor lives in Switzerland or has a representative in Switzerland, he shall be summoned to court within a short period of time and questioned.

[^190-1]: Repealed by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, with effect from 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### B. At the request of the debtor

#### Art. 191

[^191-1]

<sup>1</sup> The debtor may himself apply to be declared bankrupt by declaring to the court that he is insolvent.

<sup>2</sup> The court shall declare bankruptcy if there is no prospect of a debt settlement in accordance with Article 333 ff.

[^191-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### C. *Ex officio*

#### Art. 192

[^192-1]

Bankruptcy shall be declared *ex officio* without prior debt enforcement proceedings if the law so provides.

[^192-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### D. Against a disclaimed or overindebted estate

#### Art. 193

[^193-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The competent authority shall notify the bankruptcy court if:

- 1\. all heirs have disclaimed their inheritance or the disclaimer is presumed (Arts 566 ff. and 573 CC[^193-2]);
- 2\. an inheritance for which official liquidation has been requested or ordered proves to be overindebted (Art. 597 CC).

<sup>2</sup> In such cases, the court shall order liquidation by the bankruptcy office.

<sup>3</sup> A creditor or an heir may also request liquidation by the bankruptcy office.

[^193-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^193-2]: [SR 210](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5703)

### E. Procedure

#### Art. 194

[^194-1]

<sup>1</sup> Articles 169, 170 and 173*a*-176 apply to bankruptcies that have been declared without prior debt enforcement proceedings. However, if bankruptcy is declared in accordance with Article 192, Article 169 does not apply.

<sup>2</sup> Notification of the Commercial Register Office (Art. 176) is not required if the debtor is not subject to debt enforcement through bankruptcy.

[^194-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### IV. Revocation of Bankruptcy

### A. In general

#### Art. 195

<sup>1</sup>

The bankruptcy court shall revoke the declaration of bankruptcy and restore the debtor’s right to dispose of his assets if:

- 1\. he proves that all claims have been settled;
- 2\. he submits a written declaration from each creditor that they are withdrawing their bankruptcy petition; or
- 3\. a composition agreement has been concluded.[^195-1]

<sup>2</sup> Revocation of bankruptcy may be ordered from the expiry of the deadline for filing until the conclusion of the proceedings.

<sup>3</sup> Notice of the revocation of bankruptcy shall be published.

[^195-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### B. For a disclaimed estate

#### Art. 196

[^196-1]

The liquidation by the bankruptcy office of an estate that has been disclaimed shall also be revoked if a beneficiary declares acceptance of the inheritance before the end of the proceedings and provides sufficient security for payment of the debts.

[^196-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

## Title Six Bankruptcy Law

### I. Effects of Bankruptcy on the Debtor’s Assets

### A. Bankruptcy estate

### 1. In general

#### Art. 197

<sup>1</sup> All distrainable assets belonging to the debtor at the time that bankruptcy is declared, regardless of where they are located, form a single estate (the bankruptcy estate) which serves to satisfy the creditors jointly.[^197-1]

<sup>2</sup> Assets that accrue to the debtor[^197-2] before the end of the bankruptcy proceedings are also part of the bankruptcy estate.

[^197-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^197-2]: Term pursuant to No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)). This amendment has been made throughout the text.

### 2. Assets secured by a pledge

#### Art. 198

Assets subject to pledge shall be included in the bankruptcy estate, subject to any preferential rights granted to the pledgees.

### 3. Pledgees and attached Assets

#### Art. 199

<sup>1</sup> Assets secured by a pledge which have not been realised by the time of declaration of bankruptcy and attached assets shall be included in the bankruptcy estate.

<sup>2</sup> However, distrained cash amounts, amounts surrendered in the case of distraint proceedings of claims and income and the proceeds of assets already realised shall be distributed in accordance with Articles 144-150, provided the time limits for participating in distraint proceedings (Arts 110 and 111) have expired; any surplus falls into the bankruptcy estate.[^199-1]

[^199-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 4. Avoidance claims

#### Art. 200

The bankruptcy estate also includes any assets and claims that are subject of an action for avoidance in accordance with Articles 214 and 285-292.

### 5. Bearer securities and instruments to order

#### Art. 201

Where a bearer security or instrument to order is in the debtor’s possession but has been handed over to him or endorsed in his favour merely for collection or as cover for a specified future payment, the person who handed over or endorsed the security or instrument may demand its return.

### 6. Proceeds from the sale of items that do not belong to the debtor

#### Art. 202

If the debtor has sold an item that does not belong to him and has still to receive the purchase price at the time that bankruptcy is declared, the previous owner may, by paying what is due to the debtor, request the assignment of the claim against the purchaser or restitution of the purchase price where it has in the meantime been collected by the bankruptcy administration.

### 7. Seller’s right of return

#### Art. 203

<sup>1</sup> If an item which the debtor has purchased and not yet paid for has been dispatched to him but has not yet passed into his possession at the time that bankruptcy is declared, the seller may demand its return unless the bankruptcy administration pays the purchase price.

<sup>2</sup> However, a right of return is excluded if the item was acquired as property or a pledge by a third party acting in good faith on the basis of a waybill, bill of lading or consignment note prior to the public announcement of bankruptcy.

### B. Inability to dispose of the debtor

#### Art. 204

<sup>1</sup> Legal acts performed by the debtor after bankruptcy is declared in relation to assets belonging to the bankruptcy estate are invalid as against the bankruptcy creditors.

<sup>2</sup> However, if the debtor has paid a bill of exchange issued by him or drawn on him on the due date before the public announcement of bankruptcy, this payment is valid, provided the holder of the bill of exchange had no knowledge of the declaration of bankruptcy and could have successfully exercised recourse under the law on bills of exchange against third parties in the event of non-payment.

### C. Payments to the debtor

#### Art. 205

<sup>1</sup> Claims forming part of the bankruptcy estate may no longer be settled by payment to the debtor once bankruptcy is declared; a person who makes payment to the debtor is discharged from liability to the bankruptcy creditors only up to the amount of the sum or value which accrues to the bankruptcy estate.

<sup>2</sup> However, if the payment was made before the public announcement of bankruptcy, the payer is released from the debt obligation if he was not aware of the declaration of bankruptcy.

### D. Enforcement proceedings against the debtor

#### Art. 206

[^206-1]

<sup>1</sup> All pending debt enforcement proceedings against the debtor are cancelled, and new debt enforcement proceedings for claims that arose before bankruptcy is declared may not be initiated during the bankruptcy proceedings. This does not apply to enforcement proceedings for the realisation of assets that have been pledged by third parties.

<sup>2</sup> Enforcement proceedings for claims that arose after bankruptcy is declared shall be continued during the bankruptcy proceedings by distraint or proceedings for the realisation of pledged assets.

<sup>3</sup> During the bankruptcy proceedings, the debtor may not apply to be declared bankrupt on the grounds of insolvency (Art. 191).

[^206-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### E. Suspension of civil and administrative proceedings

#### Art. 207

[^207-1]

<sup>1</sup> With the exception of urgent cases, civil proceedings in which the debtor is a party and which affect the assets in the bankruptcy estate shall be suspended. In ordinary bankruptcy proceedings, they may be resumed at the earliest ten days after the second creditors’ meeting, and in summary bankruptcy proceedings at the earliest 20 days after the schedule of claims has been drawn up.

<sup>2</sup> Administrative proceedings may be suspended subject to the same conditions.

<sup>3</sup> During the suspension, statutes of limitation and deadlines for the forfeiture of rights are suspended.

<sup>4</sup> This provision does not apply to compensation claims for defamation and personal injury or to family law proceedings.

[^207-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### II. Effects of Bankruptcy on the Rights of Creditors

### A. Due date for debt obligations

#### Art. 208

<sup>1</sup> The declaration of bankruptcy causes all of the debtor’s debt obligations towards the bankruptcy estate to fall due, with the exception of those covered by a mortgage on the debtor’s immovable property. In addition to the principal claim, the creditor may claim interest up to the date on which bankruptcy is declared and the costs of enforcement.[^208-1]

<sup>2</sup> A five per cent discount is applied to non-interest-bearing claims that are not yet due.

[^208-1]: Amended by Art. 58 Final Title CC, in force since 1 Jan. 1912 ([AS 24 233](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/24/233_245_233)Art. 60 Final Title CC; [BBl 1904 IV 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1904/4_1_1_), [1907 VI 367](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1907/6_367__)).

### B. Interest

#### Art. 209

[^209-1]

<sup>1</sup> The debtor ceases to be liable for further interest when bankruptcy is declared.

<sup>2</sup> For claims secured by a pledge, however, interest continues to accrue until realisation provided the pledge proceeds exceed the amount of the claim and the interest accrued up to the declaration of bankruptcy.

[^209-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### C. Conditional claims

#### Art. 210

[^210-1]

<sup>1</sup> Claims subject to a condition precedent shall be admitted in bankruptcy for the full amount; however, the creditor is not entitled to receive his share of the bankruptcy estate unless the condition has been met.

<sup>2</sup> Article 518 paragraph 3 CO applies to life annuity claims[^210-2].

[^210-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^210-2]: [SR 220](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5818)

### D. Conversion of claims

#### Art. 211

<sup>1</sup> Claims that do not involve a monetary payment shall be converted into monetary claims of a corresponding value.

<sup>2</sup> However, the bankruptcy administration has the right, instead of the debtor, to perform bilateral contracts that are not or only partially performed at the time that bankruptcy is declared. The contractual partner may demand security for performance.[^211-1]

<sup>2bis</sup> However, the right of the bankruptcy administration under paragraph 2 is excluded in the case of fixed date transactions (Art. 108 para. 3 CO[^211-2]) and financial futures, swap and option transactions if the value of the contractual services can be determined on the basis of market or stock exchange prices at the time that bankruptcy is declared. The bankruptcy administration and the contractual partner each have the right to claim the difference between the agreed value of the contractual services and their market value at the time that bankruptcy is declared.[^211-3]

<sup>3</sup> The provisions of other federal laws on the termination of contractual relationships in the event of bankruptcy and the provisions on reservation of ownership (Arts 715 and 716 CC[^211-4]) remain reserved.[^211-5]

[^211-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^211-2]: [SR 220](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5818)
[^211-3]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^211-4]: [SR 210](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5703)
[^211-5]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### D<sup>bis</sup>. Long-term contractual obligations

#### Art. 211a

[^211a-1]

<sup>1</sup> Claims arising from long-term contractual obligations may be asserted as bankruptcy claims from the declaration of bankruptcy until the next possible termination date or until the end of the fixed contract term at the latest. The creditor must take into account any benefits that he has obtained for this period.

<sup>2</sup> Insofar as the bankruptcy estate has claimed the benefits from the long-term contractual obligation, the corresponding counterclaims that arose after bankruptcy is declared shall be deemed to be liabilities of the estate.

<sup>3</sup> The debtor reserves the right to continue a contractual relationship personally.

[^211a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### E. Seller’s right to withdraw

#### Art. 212

A seller who has transferred the sold item to the debtor before bankruptcy is declared may no longer withdraw from the contract and reclaim the transferred item, even if he has expressly reserved the right to do so.

### F. Offsetting

### 1. Permissibility

#### Art. 213

<sup>1</sup> A creditor may set off his claim against a claim against him to which the debtor is entitled.

<sup>2</sup>

However, offsetting is not permitted:

- 1\.[^213-1] if a debtor of the bankrupt becomes his creditor only after bankruptcy is declared, unless the debtor has fulfilled an obligation entered into beforehand or redeemed an item that is liable as a pledge for the debtor’s debt and to which it is entitled to ownership or a limited right in rem (Art. 110 no 1 CO[^213-2]);
- 2\. if a creditor of the debtor only becomes a debtor of the debtor or the bankruptcy estate after bankruptcy is declared.
- 3\.[^213-3] …

<sup>3</sup> Offsetting against claims arising from bearer securities is permitted if and insofar as the creditor proves that he acquired them in good faith prior to bankruptcy being declared.[^213-4]

<sup>4</sup> In the event of the bankruptcy of a limited partnership, a company limited by shares, a partnership limited by shares, a limited liability company or a co-operative, the partners’ specific contributions or the share capital that have not been fully paid up and contributions to the co-operative may not be offset.[^213-5] [^213-6]

[^213-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^213-2]: [SR 220](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5818)
[^213-3]: Repealed by Art. 13 of the FA of 28 Sept. 1949 (AS **1950** I 57; [BBl 1948 I 1218](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1948/1_1218_1201_)).
[^213-4]: Inserted by Art. 13 of the FA of 28 Sept. 1949, in force since 1 Feb. 1950 (AS **1950** I 57; [BBl 1948 I 1218](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1948/1_1218_1201_)).
[^213-5]: Originally para. 3.
[^213-6]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Contestability

#### Art. 214

Offsetting may be contested if a debtor of the bankrupt[^214-1] has acquired a claim against the bankrupt prior to bankruptcy being declared but with knowledge of the bankrupt's insolvency in order to obtain an advantage for himself or another person by means of offsetting, to the detriment of the bankruptcy estate.

[^214-1]: Term pursuant to No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)). This amendment has been made throughout the text.

### G. Co-obligations of the debtor

### 1. Contracts of surety

#### Art. 215

<sup>1</sup> Claims arising from contracts of surety entered into by the debtor may be asserted in bankruptcy, even if they are not yet due.

<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy estate is subrogated to the creditor's rights as against the principal debtor and any co-sureties in respect of the amount that it pays (Art. 507 CO[^215-1]). However, if bankruptcy proceedings are also instituted against the principal debtor or a co-surety, Articles 216 and 217 apply.[^215-2]

[^215-1]: [SR 220](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5818)
[^215-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Simultaneous bankruptcy of several co-obligors

#### Art. 216

<sup>1</sup> If bankruptcy is declared simultaneously against two or more co-obligors, the creditor may assert his claim in full in each bankruptcy.

<sup>2</sup> If the allocations from the various bankruptcy estates exceed the amount of the entire claim, the surplus shall revert to the estates in accordance with the rights of recourse existing among the co-obligors.

<sup>3</sup> If the total amount of the allocations does not reach the full amount of the claim, the estates have no recourse against each other for any instalments paid.

### 3. Instalments paid by co-obligors

#### Art. 217

<sup>1</sup> If a creditor has received partial satisfaction of his claim from a co-obligor of the debtor, the claim shall nevertheless be included in the bankruptcy of the latter in its full original amount, irrespective of whether the co-obligor is entitled to recourse against the debtor or not.

<sup>2</sup> The creditor and the co-obligor are both entitled to file the claim in bankruptcy.

<sup>3</sup> The creditor is entitled to a share of the bankruptcy estate attributable to the claim until it has been satisfied in full. A co-obligor entitled to recourse receives the amount from the surplus that he would receive if he had asserted his right of recourse independently. The residue remains for the estates.

### 4. Bankruptcy of general and limited partnerships and their partners

#### Art. 218

<sup>1</sup> If a general partnership and any of its partners are simultaneously declared bankrupt, the creditors of the partnership may only assert in the bankruptcy of the partner that part of their claims that remained unpaid in the bankruptcy of the partnership. The provisions of Articles 216 and 217 apply with regard to the payment of this residual debt by the individual partners.

<sup>2</sup> If bankruptcy proceedings are instituted against a partner but not simultaneously against the partnership, the creditors of the partnership may assert their claims in full in the bankruptcy of the partner. The bankruptcy estate is entitled to the rights of recourse granted by Article 215 to the bankruptcy estate of a surety.

<sup>3</sup> Paragraphs 1 and 2 apply *mutatis mutandis* to partners with unlimited liability in a limited partnership.[^218-1]

[^218-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### H. Ranking of creditors

#### Art. 219

<sup>1</sup> Claims secured by a pledge are paid in advance from the proceeds of the sale of the pledges.

<sup>2</sup> If several pledges secure the same claim, the amounts realised therefrom shall be used to cover the claim in proportion to their amount.

<sup>3</sup> The ranking of mortgagees and the extent of mortgage security for interest and other ancillary claims shall be determined in accordance with the provisions governing mortgages.[^219-subdivu1-1]

<sup>4</sup> Claims not secured by a pledge and the unsecured portion of claims secured by a pledge shall be covered from the proceeds of the entire remaining bankruptcy estate in the following order of priority:

[^219-subdivu1-1]: Amended by Art. 58 Final Title CC, in force since 1 Jan. 1912 ([AS 24 233](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/24/233_245_233)Art. 60 Final Title CC; [BBl 1904 IV 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1904/4_1_1_), [1907 VI 367](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1907/6_367__)).

### First class

- a.[^219-subdivu2-1] The claims of employees arising from the employment relationship that arose or became due no earlier than six months prior to bankruptcy being declared, but up to a maximum of the maximum insured annual salary under the compulsory accident insurance.
- a<sup>bis</sup>.[^219-subdivu2-2] The refund of employee deposits.
- a<sup>ter</sup>.[^219-subdivu2-3] Employee claims arising from social plans that arose or became due no earlier than six months before bankruptcy is declared.
- b. The claims of insured persons under the Federal Act of 20 March 1981[^219-subdivu2-4] on Accident Insurance and from non-mandatory occupational benefit schemes and the claims of employee benefit schemes as against affiliated employers.
- c.[^219-subdivu2-5] Maintenance and support claims under family law and maintenance contributions under the Same-Sex Partnership Act of 18 June 2004[^219-subdivu2-6] that arose in the six months prior to bankruptcy being declared and are to be satisfied by monetary payments.

[^219-subdivu2-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 18 June 2010, in force since 1 Dec. 2010 ([AS 2010 4921](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/688); [BBl 2009 7979](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/1398)[7989](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/1400)). See also the transitional provision at the end of the text.
[^219-subdivu2-2]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 18 June 2010, in force since 1 Dec. 2010 ([AS 2010 4921](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/688); [BBl 2009 7979](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/1398)[7989](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/1400)). See also the transitional provision at the end of the text.
[^219-subdivu2-3]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 18 June 2010, in force since 1 Dec. 2010 ([AS 2010 4921](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/688); [BBl 2009 7979](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/1398)[7989](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/1400)). See also the transitional provision at the end of the text.
[^219-subdivu2-4]: [SR 832.20](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/10190)
[^219-subdivu2-5]: Amended by Annex No 16 of the Same-Sex Partnership Act of 18 June 2004, in force since 1 Jan. 2007 ([AS 2005 5685](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2005/782); [BBl 2003 1288](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2003/194)).
[^219-subdivu2-6]: [SR 211.231](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5750)

### Second class

[^219-subdivu3-1]

[^219-subdivu3-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 24 March 2000, in force since 1 Jan. 2001 ([AS 2000 2531](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2000/401); [BBl 1999 9126](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1999/1_9126_8486_8077)[9547](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1999/1_9547_8886_8458)).

- a. The claims of persons whose assets were entrusted to the debtor by virtue of parental authority for everything that the debtor owed them in this capacity. This preferential right only applies if notice of bankruptcy was given while the assets were under parental administration or within one year of its end.
- b. Contribution claims under the Federal Act of 20 December 1946[^219-subdivu3-2] on Old Age and Survivors’ Insurance, the Federal Act of 19 June 1959[^219-subdivu3-3] on Invalidity Insurance, the Federal Act of 20 March 1981 on Accident Insurance, the Loss of Earnings Compensation Act of 25 September 1952[^219-subdivu3-4] and the Unemployment Insurance Act of 25 June 1982[^219-subdivu3-5].
- c. Claims in respect of premiums and cost contributions under the social health insurance.
- d. Contributions to the family compensation fund.
- e.[^219-subdivu3-6] ...
- f.[^219-subdivu3-7] Contributions pursuant to Article 37*a* of the Banking Act of 8 November 1934[^219-subdivu3-8].

[^219-subdivu3-2]: [SR 831.10](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/10002)
[^219-subdivu3-3]: [SR 831.20](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/10046)
[^219-subdivu3-4]: [SR 834.1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/10262)
[^219-subdivu3-5]: [SR 837.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/10282)
[^219-subdivu3-6]: Inserted by Art. 111 no 2 of the Value Added Tax Act of 12 June 2009 ([AS 2009 5203](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2009/615); [BBl 2008 6885](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2008/1202)). Repealed by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, with effect from 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).
[^219-subdivu3-7]: Inserted by Annex No 2 of the FA of 18 March 2011 (Safeguarding of Deposits), in force since 1 Sept. 2011 ([AS 2011 3919](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2011/559); [BBl 2010 3993](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/655)).
[^219-subdivu3-8]: [SR 952.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/11561)

### Third class

All other claims.[^219-subdivu4-1]

<sup>5</sup>

The following are not taken into account when calculating the deadlines set in the first and second classes:

- 1\. the duration of previous composition proceedings;
- 2\. the duration of court proceedings relating to the claim;
- 3\. in the case of the liquidation of a deceased’s estate by the bankruptcy office, the period between the date of death and the order for liquidation.[^219-subdivu4-2]

[^219-subdivu4-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^219-subdivu4-2]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)). Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### I. Relationship between the classes

#### Art. 220

<sup>1</sup> The creditors of the same class have equal rights among themselves.

<sup>2</sup> The creditors of a lower class are only entitled to the proceeds once the creditors of the preceding class have been satisfied.

## Title Seven Bankruptcy Proceedings

### I. Establishing the Bankruptcy Estate and Deciding on the Procedure

[^tit7-lvlI-1]

[^tit7-lvlI-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### A. Inventory

#### Art. 221

<sup>1</sup> Immediately on receipt of the declaration of bankruptcy, the bankruptcy office shall proceed to record an inventory of the assets belonging to the bankruptcy estate and take the measures necessary to secure them.

<sup>2</sup> …[^221-1]

[^221-1]: Repealed by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, with effect from 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### B. Duty to provide information on and hand over assets

#### Art. 222

[^222-1]

<sup>1</sup> The debtor is obliged, subject to criminal penalties for failing to do so, to declare and hand over all his assets to the bankruptcy office (Arts 163 para. 1 and 323 para. 4 SCC[^222-2]).

<sup>2</sup> If the debtor has died or has absconded, all adult persons who lived in the same household as the debtor are subject to the same obligations, subject to criminal penalties for failing to do so (Art. 324 para. 1 SCC).

<sup>3</sup> The persons obliged under paragraphs 1 and 2 must open the premises and containers to the official on request. The official may use police powers if necessary.

<sup>4</sup> Third parties who hold the debtor’s assets in safekeeping or with whom the debtor has assets are obliged to provide information on and hand over the assets to the same extent as the debtor, subject to criminal penalties for failing to do so (Art. 324 para. 5 SCC).

<sup>5</sup> Public authorities are obliged to provide information to the same extent as the debtor.

<sup>6</sup> The bankruptcy office shall expressly draw the attention of the persons concerned to their obligations and to the criminal penalties for failing to comply.

[^222-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^222-2]: [SR 311.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6196)

### B<sup>bis</sup>. Delivery and opening of post

#### Art. 222a

[^222a-1]

<sup>1</sup> The bankruptcy office may instruct postal services providers to allow it to inspect post addressed the debtor for the duration of the bankruptcy proceedings and to deliver the post to the bankruptcy office.

<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy office is entitled to open the post delivered provided it is not obvious that the content of the post is of no relevance to the bankruptcy proceedings.

<sup>3</sup> The debtor is entitled to be present when post is opened.

[^222a-1]: Inserted by No I 2 of the FA of 18 March 2022 on Combating Bankruptcy Fraud, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2023 628](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/628); [BBl 2019 5193](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2019/1847)).

### C. Protective measures

#### Art. 223

<sup>1</sup> Stores, warehouses, workshops, public houses and the like must be closed immediately by the bankruptcy office and placed under seal if they cannot be managed under adequate supervision until the first creditors’ meeting.

<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy office shall take custody of cash, securities, business and household accounts and other relevant documents.

<sup>3</sup> All other assets shall be placed under seal unless they are recorded in the inventory; the assets may be resealed after their recording if the bankruptcy office deems it necessary.

<sup>4</sup> The bankruptcy office shall ensure the safekeeping of items located outside the premises used by the debtor.

### D. Items essential for subsistence

#### Art. 224

The assets referred to in Article 92 shall be left at the debtor’s free disposal but shall nevertheless be recorded in the inventory.

### E. Rights of third parties

### 1. To chattels

#### Art. 225

Items designated as the property of third parties or claimed by third parties as their property shall nevertheless be recorded in the inventory, with the claim being noted.

### 2. To immovable property

#### Art. 226

[^226-1]

The rights of third parties to the debtor’s immovable property as recorded in the land register shall be noted *ex officio* in the inventory.

[^226-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### F. Valuation

#### Art. 227

The estimated value of each asset is recorded in the inventory.

### G. Declaration of the debtor on the inventory

#### Art. 228

<sup>1</sup> The inventory shall be presented to the debtor with the request that it be declared complete and accurate.

<sup>2</sup> The debtor’s declaration shall be recorded in the inventory and signed by the debtor.

### H. Debtor’s cooperation and support

#### Art. 229

<sup>1</sup> The debtor is obliged to remain at the disposal of the bankruptcy administration during the bankruptcy proceedings subject to criminal penalties for failing to do so (Art. 323 no 5 SCC[^229-1]); he may only be released from this obligation by special authorisation. If necessary, he shall be brought to the required location with the aid of police powers. The bankruptcy administration shall expressly draw his attention to this and to the penalties for failing to comply.[^229-2]
