<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy administration may grant the debtor a reasonable contribution towards his support, in particular if this encourages the debtor to remain at its disposal.

<sup>3</sup> The bankruptcy administration shall determine how and under what terms the debtor and his family may remain in their current home, in the event that it is part of the bankruptcy estate.[^229-3]

[^229-1]: [SR 311.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6196)
[^229-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^229-3]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### I. Discontinuation of bankruptcy proceedings due to lack of assets

### 1. In general

#### Art. 230

<sup>1</sup> If the bankruptcy estate is unlikely to be sufficient to cover the costs of summary proceedings, the bankruptcy court shall order the bankruptcy proceedings to be discontinued at the request of the bankruptcy office.[^230-1]

<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy office shall give public notice of discontinuation and notify the known creditors by standard post. In the notice, it shall indicate the proceedings will be concluded unless a creditor requests the conducted of bankruptcy proceedings within 20 days and provides the security specified for the portion of the costs not covered by the bankruptcy estate.[^230-2]

<sup>3</sup> After the bankruptcy proceedings have been discontinued, the debtor may still be pursued by way of distraint for a period of two years.[^230-3]

<sup>4</sup> Debt enforcement proceedings initiated prior to bankruptcy being declared shall be revived after the bankruptcy proceedings have been discontinued. The time between the opening and the discontinuation of bankruptcy proceedings shall not be counted for the purposes of any deadlines under this Act.[^230-4]

[^230-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^230-2]: Amended by No I 2 of the FA of 18 March 2022 on Combating Bankruptcy Fraud, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2023 628](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/628); [BBl 2019 5193](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2019/1847)).
[^230-3]: Inserted by Art. 15 of the FA of 28 Sept. 1949, in force since 1 Feb. 1950 (AS **1950** I 57; [BBl 1948 I 1218](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1948/1_1218_1201_)).
[^230-4]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. In the case of a disclaimed estate and for legal entities

#### Art. 230a

[^230a-1]

<sup>1</sup> If the bankruptcy liquidation of a deceased’s estate is discontinued due to a lack of assets, the heirs may request assignment of the assets belonging to the estate to the community of heirs or to individual heirs if they declare their willingness to assume the personal debt obligation for the claims secured by a pledge and to pay the uncovered liquidation costs. If none of the heirs exercises this right, the creditors and, after them, third parties who assert an interest may exercise it.

<sup>2</sup> If there are assets secured by a pledge in the bankruptcy estate of a legal entity and the bankruptcy has been discontinued due to a lack of assets, a pledgee may nevertheless request the bankruptcy office to realise their pledge. The office shall set a deadline for this.

<sup>3</sup> If no assignment agreement within the meaning of paragraph 1 is concluded and no creditor demands the realisation of his pledge in due time, the assets shall be transferred to the state after deduction of the costs, together with the encumbrances liable thereon, but without the personal debt obligation, unless the competent cantonal authority rejects the transfer.

<sup>4</sup> If the competent cantonal authority rejects the transfer, the bankruptcy office shall realise the assets.

[^230a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### K. Summary bankruptcy proceedings

#### Art. 231

[^231-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The bankruptcy office shall apply to the bankruptcy court for summary proceedings if it decides that:

- 1\. the costs of ordinary bankruptcy proceedings are unlikely to be covered by the proceeds from the inventoried assets; or
- 2\. the case is straightforward.

<sup>2</sup> If the court shares the opinion of the bankruptcy office, the bankruptcy shall be conducted in summary proceedings unless a creditor requests ordinary proceedings before the proceeds are distributed and provides adequate security for the anticipated uncovered costs.

<sup>3</sup>

Summary bankruptcy proceedings shall be conducted in accordance with the provisions governing ordinary proceedings, subject to the following exceptions:

- Creditors’ meetings are generally not convened. However, if it appears desirable to hear the creditors because of special circumstances, the bankruptcy office may invite them to a meeting or obtain a creditor resolution by circular letter.
- 2\. On expiry of the time limit for filing (Art. 232 para. 2 no 2), the bankruptcy office shall carry out the realisation; it shall take account of Article 256 paragraphs 2-4 and safeguard the interests of creditors as far as possible. It may only realise immovable property once the list of encumbrances has been drawn up.
- 3\. The bankruptcy office shall designate the items essential for subsistence in the inventory and publish it together with the schedule of claims.
- 4\. the distribution list need not be published.

[^231-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### II. Call to Creditors

[^tit7-lvlII-1]

[^tit7-lvlII-1]: Originally before Art. 231.

### A. Public notice

#### Art. 232

<sup>1</sup> The bankruptcy office shall give public notice of the declaration of bankruptcy as soon as it has been decided whether it will be conducted as ordinary or summary proceedings.[^232-1]

<sup>2</sup>

The notice shall contain:

- 1\. the name of the debtor and his place of residence as well as the date of declaration of bankruptcy;
- 2\.[^232-2] the request for the debtor’s creditors and all those who have claims to the assets in the debtor’s possession to submit their claims or demands to the bankruptcy office within one month of the announcement, together with documentary evidence thereof (promissory notes, account extracts, etc.);
- 3\.[^232-3] the request for the bankrupt's debtors to report to the bankruptcy office within the same period, together with a reference to the criminal penalty for failure to do so (Art. 324 no 2 SCC[^232-4]);
- 4\.[^232-5] the request for persons who hold the debtor’s assets as pledgees or for other reasons to make these assets available to the bankruptcy office within the same period, as well as a reference to the criminal penalty for failure to do so (Art. 324 para. 3 SCC) and to the fact that their preferential right expires if they fail to give notice without justification;
- 5\.[^232-6] the invitation to a first creditors’ meeting, which must take place no later than 20 days after the public announcement and which may also be attended by co-debtors and the debtor’s sureties as well as persons subject to warranty obligations;
- 6\.[^232-7] information that for parties residing abroad, the bankruptcy office is deemed to be the place of service unless they designate another place of service in Switzerland.

[^232-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^232-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^232-3]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^232-4]: [SR 311.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6196)
[^232-5]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^232-6]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^232-7]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### B. Special notice to creditors

#### Art. 233

[^233-1]

The bankruptcy office shall send a copy of the notice by standard post to each creditor whose name and place of residence are known.

[^233-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### C. Special cases

#### Art. 234

[^234-1]

If a call to creditors has already taken place prior to the liquidation of a deceased’s estate that has been disclaimed or in composition proceedings prior to bankruptcy, the bankruptcy office shall set the deadline for filing claims at ten days and state in the notice that creditors who have already given notice do not have to do so again.

[^234-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)). The correction of the Federal Assembly Drafting Committee of 14 March 2017, published on 28 March 2017 only concerns the Italian text ([AS 2017 2165](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/196)).

### III. Administration

### A. First creditors’ meeting

### 1. Constitution and quorum

#### Art. 235

<sup>1</sup> At the first creditors’ meeting, a bankruptcy officer shall chair the proceedings and forms the office with two creditors that he appoints.

<sup>2</sup> The office shall decide on the admission of persons who wish to participate in the discussions without being specifically invited.

<sup>3</sup> The meeting is quorate if at least one quarter of the known creditors are present or represented. If four or fewer creditors are present or represented, a valid hearing may be held provided that they account for at least half of the known creditors.

<sup>4</sup> The meeting passes resolutions by an absolute majority of the creditors voting. In the event of a tie, the chair has the casting vote. If the calculation of votes is disputed, the office shall decide.[^235-1]

[^235-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Inability to pass resolutions

#### Art. 236

[^236-1]

If the meeting is not quorate, the bankruptcy office shall formally establish this. It shall inform the creditors present about the extent of the estate and administer it until the second creditors’ meeting.

[^236-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3. Powers

### a. Appointment of the bankruptcy administration and creditors’ committee

#### Art. 237

<sup>1</sup> If the creditors’ meeting is quorate, the bankruptcy office shall report to it on the recording of the inventory and the status of the estate.

<sup>2</sup> The meeting shall decide whether it wishes to appoint the bankruptcy office or one or more persons that it chooses to be the bankruptcy administration.

<sup>3</sup>

In either case, the meeting may elect a creditors’ committee from among its members; unless the meeting decides otherwise, this committee has the following duties:[^237-1]

- 1\. supervising the activities of the bankruptcy administration, assessing the matters submitted by the latter, objecting to any measure contrary to the interests of the creditors;
- 2\. authorising the trade or business operated by the bankrupt to continue and deciding on the conditions for doing so;
- 3\. approving invoices, granting authorisation to conduct legal proceedings and to conclude settlements and arbitration agreements;
- 4\. rejecting claims on the bankruptcy estate which the administration has approved;
- 5\. ordering interim distributions to the bankruptcy creditors in the course of the bankruptcy proceedings.

[^237-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### b. Resolutions on urgent matters

#### Art. 238

<sup>1</sup> The creditors’ meeting may pass resolutions on matters whose settlement cannot be delayed, in particular on the continuation of the bankrupt's trade or business, on the question of whether the bankrupt's workshops, warehouses or business premises should remain open, on the continuation of pending legal proceedings, and on the conduct of private sales[^238-1].

<sup>2</sup> If the bankrupt proposes a composition agreement, the creditors’ meeting may suspend realisation.

[^238-1]: Term pursuant to No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)). This amendment has been made throughout the text.

### 4. Appeal

#### Art. 239

<sup>1</sup> An appeal may be filed with the supervisory authority within five days against resolutions of the creditors’ meeting.[^239-1]

<sup>2</sup> The supervisory authority shall decide within a short period of time after consulting the bankruptcy office and, if it deems it appropriate, the appellant and those creditors who request to be heard.

[^239-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### B. Bankruptcy administration

### 1. Tasks in general

#### Art. 240

The bankruptcy administration is responsible for all transactions relating to the preservation and realisation of the estate; it represents the estate in court.

### 2. Status of the non-official bankruptcy administration

#### Art. 241

[^241-1]

Articles 8-11, 13, 14 paragraph 2 numbers 1, 2 and 4 as well as Articles 17-19, 34 and 35 also apply to a non-official bankruptcy administration.

[^241-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3. Segregation and adding to the estate

#### Art. 242

[^242-1]

<sup>1</sup> The bankruptcy administration shall issue an order for items claimed by a third party to be surrendered.

<sup>2</sup> If the bankruptcy administration considers the claim to be unfounded, it shall set the third party a time limit of 20 days within which he may file an action in the court at the place of bankruptcy. If he does not comply with this deadline, the claim is forfeited.

<sup>3</sup> If the estate claims movable items that are in the custody or joint custody of a third party, or immovable property that is recorded in the land register in the name of a third party, as the property of the debtor, it must bring an action against the third party.

[^242-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3*a*. Surrender of crypto-based assets

#### Art. 242a

[^242a-1]

<sup>1</sup> The bankruptcy administration shall issue an order for the surrender of crypto-based assets over which the bankrupt has power of disposal at the time that bankruptcy is declared and which are claimed by a third party.

<sup>2</sup>

The claim is justified if the bankrupt has undertaken to hold the crypto-based assets ready for the third party at all times and the assets:

- a. are individually assigned to the third party; or
- b. are allocated to a community and the share of the community assets to which the third party is entitled is clear.

<sup>3</sup> If the bankruptcy administration considers the claim to be unfounded, it shall set the third party a time limit of 20 days within which it may file an action in the court at the place of bankruptcy. If he does not comply with this deadline, the claim is forfeited.

<sup>4</sup> The costs of surrender shall be borne by the party requesting the same. The bankruptcy administration may demand a suitable advance payment.

[^242a-1]: Inserted by No I 2 of the FA of 25 Sept. 2020 on the Adaptation of Federal Law to Developments in the Technology of Distributed Electronic Registers, in force since 1 Aug. 2021 ([AS 2021 33](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/33), [399](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/399); [BBl 2020 233](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2020/16)).

### 3*b*. Access to and disclosure of data

#### Art. 242b

[^242b-1]

<sup>1</sup> If data fall under the power of disposal of the bankruptcy estate, any third party who can prove a legal or contractual entitlement to the data may, depending on the type of entitlement, demand access to this data or its release from the power of disposal of the bankruptcy estate.

<sup>2</sup> If the bankruptcy administration considers the claim to be unfounded, it shall set the third party a deadline of 20 days within which it may file an action in the court at the place of bankruptcy. The data may not be destroyed or used until the court has issued a legally binding decision.

<sup>3</sup> The costs for access to the data or for their release shall be borne by the party requesting access. The bankruptcy administration may demand a suitable advance payment.

<sup>4</sup> The right to information in accordance with the federal or cantonal data protection provisions remains reserved.

[^242b-1]: Inserted by No I 2 of the FA of 25 Sept. 2020 on the Adaptation of Federal Law to Developments in the Technology of Distributed Electronic Registers, in force since 1 Aug. 2021 ([AS 2021 33](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/33), [399](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/399); [BBl 2020 233](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2020/16)).

### 4. Collection of claims. Emergency sale

#### Art. 243

<sup>1</sup> Undisputed assets due to the estate shall be collected by the bankruptcy administration, if necessary by way of debt enforcement proceedings.

<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy administration shall realise without delay assets that are subject to rapid depreciation, require costly maintenance or involve disproportionately high storage costs. It may also order that securities and other items with a stock exchange or market price be realised immediately.[^243-1]

<sup>3</sup> The remaining parts of the estate shall be realised after the second creditors’ meeting has taken place.

[^243-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### IV. Verification of the Bankruptcy Claims. Ranking of Creditor’s Claims

### A. Examination of the claims submitted

#### Art. 244

After expiry of the deadline for filing, the bankruptcy administration shall examine the claims filed and make the necessary enquiries for their verification. It shall consult the bankruptcy debtor about each bankruptcy claim filed.

### B. Decision

#### Art. 245

The bankruptcy administration shall decide on whether to recognise the claims. It is not bound by the declaration of the bankruptcy debtor.

### C. *Ex officio* inclusion

#### Art. 246

[^246-1]

The claims evident in the land register shall be included among the bankruptcy claims together with the current interest, even if they have not been filed.

[^246-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### D. Schedule of claims

### 1. Creation

#### Art. 247

[^247-1]

<sup>1</sup> The bankruptcy administration shall draw up the plan for the ranking of creditors (schedule of claims, Arts 219 and 220) within 60 days of the expiry of the time limit for filing claims.

<sup>2</sup> If the estate includes immovable property, the bankruptcy administration shall draw up a list of the encumbrances (mortgages, easements, charges on land and registered personal rights) within the same period. The list of encumbrances forms part of the schedule of claims.

<sup>3</sup> If a creditors’ committee has been appointed, the bankruptcy administration shall submit the schedule of claims and the list of encumbrances to it for approval; the committee may make changes within ten days.

<sup>4</sup> The supervisory authority may extend the deadlines specified in this Article if necessary.

[^247-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Rejected claims

#### Art. 248

Rejected claims shall also be noted in the schedule of claims, together with the reason for rejection.

### 3. Publication and special notices

#### Art. 249

<sup>1</sup> The schedule of claims shall be made available for inspection at the bankruptcy office.

<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy administration shall give public notice that the schedule is available for inspection[^249-1].

<sup>3</sup> Each creditor whose claim has been rejected in whole or in part or who has not been given the rank requested shall be notified separately of the publication of the schedule of claims and the rejection of his claim or request.

[^249-1]: Term pursuant to No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)). This amendment has been made throughout the text.

### 4. Action to contest the schedule of claims

#### Art. 250

[^250-1]

<sup>1</sup> Any creditor who wishes to contest the schedule of claims because his claim has been rejected in whole or in part or has not been given the requested ranking must file an action against the estate in the court at the place of bankruptcy within 20 days of the publication of the schedule of claims.

<sup>2</sup> If he wishes to contest the admission or ranking of another creditor, he must bring the action against that creditor. If the court approves the claim, the amount by which the defendant’s share of the bankruptcy estate is reduced shall serve to satisfy the plaintiff’s claim up to its full amount, including the legal costs. Any surplus shall be distributed in accordance with the revised schedule of claims.

<sup>3</sup> …[^250-2]

[^250-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^250-2]: Repealed by Annex 1 no II 17 of the Civil Procedure Code of 19 Dec. 2008, with effect from 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 1739](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/262); [BBl 2006 7221](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/914)).

### 5. Late filing of claims

#### Art. 251

<sup>1</sup> Bankruptcy claims may be filed until the conclusion of the bankruptcy proceedings.

<sup>2</sup> The creditor must bear any costs caused by the delay and may be required to make a suitable advance payment.

<sup>3</sup> The creditor shall not be entitled to provisional distributions that took place prior to his filing a claim.

<sup>4</sup> If the bankruptcy administration considers a bankruptcy claim filed late to be justified, it shall amend the schedule of claims and give public notice of the amendment.

<sup>5</sup> Article 250 applies.

### V. Realisation

### A. Second creditors’ meeting

### 1. Invitation

#### Art. 252

<sup>1</sup> After the schedule of claims has been published, the bankruptcy administration shall invite the creditors whose claims have not already been legally rejected to a second meeting. The invitation must be sent out at least 20 days before the meeting.[^252-1]

<sup>2</sup> If a composition agreement is to be negotiated at this meeting, this shall be indicated in the invitation.[^252-2]

<sup>3</sup> A member of the bankruptcy administration shall chair the meeting. Article 235 paragraphs 3 and 4 apply *mutatis mutandis*.

[^252-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^252-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Powers

#### Art. 253

<sup>1</sup> The bankruptcy administration shall submit a comprehensive report to the creditors’ meeting on the progress of the administration and the status of the assets and liabilities.

<sup>2</sup> The meeting shall decide on the confirmation of the bankruptcy administration and, where appropriate, of the creditors’ committee and shall, without limitation, order whatever else is required in order to conduct the bankruptcy proceedings.

### 3. Inability to pass resolutions

#### Art. 254

[^254-1]

If the meeting is not quorate, the bankruptcy office shall formally establish this and inform the creditors present of the status of the assets. The current bankruptcy administration and the creditors’ committee shall remain in office until the end of the proceedings.

[^254-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### B. Further creditors’ meetings

#### Art. 255

[^255-1]

Further creditors’ meetings shall be convened if a quarter of the creditors or the creditors’ committee so request or if the bankruptcy administration deems it necessary.

[^255-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### C. Circular resolution

#### Art. 255a

[^255a-1]

<sup>1</sup> In urgent cases, or if a creditors’ meeting was not quorate, the bankruptcy administration may submit proposals to the creditors by circular letter. A proposal shall be accepted if the majority of the creditors expressly or tacitly approve it within the set deadline.

<sup>2</sup> If not all creditors are known to the bankruptcy administration, it may also give public notice of its proposals.

[^255a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### D. Method of realisation

#### Art. 256

<sup>1</sup> The assets belonging to the estate shall be sold at public auction by order of the bankruptcy administration or, if the creditors so decide, by private sale.

<sup>2</sup> Unless they are sold at public auction, assets secured by a pledge may only be realised with the consent of the pledgee.

<sup>3</sup> Assets of significant value and immovable property may only be sold privately if the creditors have first been given the opportunity to make higher offers.[^256-1]

<sup>4</sup> Avoidance claims under Articles 286-288 may not be auctioned or otherwise disposed of.[^256-2]

[^256-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^256-2]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### E. Auction

### 1. Public announcement

#### Art. 257

<sup>1</sup> The place, day and hour of the increase shall be publicly announced.

<sup>2</sup> If immovable property is to be realised, notice shall be given at least one month before the date of the auction and shall state the date from which the auction terms will be available for inspection at the bankruptcy office.

<sup>3</sup> Copies of the notice shall be sent separately to the mortgagees, stating the amount of the valuation.

### 2. Sale

#### Art. 258

[^258-1]

<sup>1</sup> The item to be realised shall be sold to the highest bidder after three calls.

<sup>2</sup> Article 142 paragraphs 1 and 3 applies to the realisation of immovable property. The creditors may also decide to set a minimum bid for the first auction.[^258-2]

[^258-1]: Amended by Art. 16 of the FA of 28 Sept. 1949, in force since 1 Feb. 1950 (AS **1950** I 57; [BBl 1948 I 1218](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1948/1_1218_1201_)).
[^258-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 3. Auction terms

#### Art. 259

[^259-1]

Articles 128, 129, 132*a*, 134-137 and 143 apply *mutatis mutandis* to the auction terms. The debt enforcement office is replaced by the bankruptcy administration.

[^259-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### F. Assignment of rights

#### Art. 260

<sup>1</sup> If the community of creditors waives its right to assert a claim, any one of the creditors may individually request that the estate’s rights be assigned to him.

<sup>2</sup> After deduction of the costs, the result shall serve to cover the claims of those creditors to which the claim was assigned, in accordance with their respective ranks. The surplus must be paid into the estate.

<sup>3</sup> If the community of creditors waives its right to assert its claims and no creditor demands assignment, such claims may be realised in accordance with Article 256.[^260-1]

[^260-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### VI. Distribution

### A. Distribution list and final account

#### Art. 261

After receipt of the proceeds of the entire bankruptcy estate and after the schedule of claims has become legally binding, the bankruptcy administration shall draw up the distribution list and the final account.

### B. Procedural costs

#### Art. 262

[^262-1]

<sup>1</sup> All costs for opening and conducting the bankruptcy proceedings and for recording an inventory of assets shall be covered beforehand.

<sup>2</sup> Only the costs of inventorying, managing and realising are covered from the proceeds from assets secured by a pledge.

[^262-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### C. Publication of the distribution list and final account

#### Art. 263

<sup>1</sup> The distribution list and the final account shall be made available for inspection at the bankruptcy office for ten days.

<sup>2</sup> Each creditor shall receive notice of publication, enclosing an extract relating to his share.

### D. Distribution

#### Art. 264

<sup>1</sup> The bankruptcy administration shall proceed with the distribution immediately after the expiry of the period allowed to inspect the distribution list and the final account.

<sup>2</sup> The provisions of Article 150 apply accordingly.

<sup>3</sup> The shares attributable to the claims subject to a condition precedent or with an uncertain expiry date shall be deposited with the official depositary.

### E. Unpaid debt certificate

### 1. Content and effects

#### Art. 265

<sup>1</sup> During distribution, each creditor shall receive an unpaid debt certificate for the amount of his claim that remains uncovered. The certificate shall state whether the claim has been accepted or contested by the debtor. In the former case, the unpaid debt certificate is deemed to be an acknowledgement of debt within the meaning of Article 82.

<sup>2</sup> The unpaid debt certificate entitles the holder to carry out an attachment and has the legal effects specified in Articles 149 paragraph 4 and 149*a*. However, new debt enforcement proceedings may only be initiated on the basis of the certificate if the debtor has acquired new assets. New assets also include assets over which the debtor has economic control.[^265-1]

<sup>3</sup> …[^265-2]

[^265-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^265-2]: Repealed by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, with effect from 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Establishing new assets

#### Art. 265a

[^265a-1]

<sup>1</sup> If the debtor files an objection to the summons for payment on the grounds that he has not acquired new assets, the debt enforcement office shall submit the objection to the court at the place of debt enforcement. The court shall hear the parties and issue a decision; there is no right of appeal against the decision.[^265a-2]

<sup>2</sup> The court shall approve the objection to the summons if the debtor sets out his income and financial circumstances and credibly demonstrates that he has not acquired any new assets.

<sup>3</sup> If the court does not approve the objection to the summons, it shall establish the extent of the new assets (Art. 265 para. 2). The court may declare assets of third parties over which the debtor has economic control to be distrainable if the third party's right is based on an act carried out by the debtor with the intention, recognisable to the third party, of thwarting the creation of new assets.

<sup>4</sup> The debtor and the creditor may file an action to contest or establish the new assets in the court at the place of debt enforcement within 20 days of receiving notice of the decision on the objection.[^265a-3]

[^265a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^265a-2]: Amended by Annex 1 no II 17 of the Civil Procedure Code of 19 Dec. 2008, in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 1739](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/262); [BBl 2006 7221](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/914)).
[^265a-3]: Amended by Annex 1 no II 17 of the Civil Procedure Code of 19 Dec. 2008, in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 1739](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/262); [BBl 2006 7221](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/914)).

### 3. Exclusion of declaration of bankruptcy at the request of the debtor

#### Art. 265b

[^265b-1]

If the debtor opposes debt enforcement proceedings by denying that he has new assets, he may not apply to be declared bankrupt (Art. 191) for the duration of these proceedings.

[^265b-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### F. Provisional distributions

#### Art. 266

<sup>1</sup> Provisional distributions may be made as soon as the deadline for contesting the schedule of claims has expired.

<sup>2</sup> Article 263 applies *mutatis mutandis*.[^266-1]

[^266-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### G. Claims not filed

#### Art. 267

The claims of those creditors who have not participated in the bankruptcy proceedings are subject to the same restrictions as those for which an unpaid debt certificate has been issued.

### VII. Conclusion of the Bankruptcy Proceedings

### A. Final report and decision of the bankruptcy court

#### Art. 268

<sup>1</sup> After distribution, the bankruptcy administration shall submit a final report to the bankruptcy court.

<sup>2</sup> If the court finds that the bankruptcy proceedings have been completed, it shall declare them concluded.

<sup>3</sup> If the manner in which the bankruptcy has been managed by the administration gives rise to comment by the court, the court shall bring the matter to the attention of the supervisory authority.

<sup>4</sup> The bankruptcy office shall give public notice of the conclusion of the bankruptcy proceedings.

### B. Assets discovered subsequently

#### Art. 269

<sup>1</sup> If, after the conclusion of bankruptcy proceedings, assets are discovered that belonged to the estate but were not included therein, the bankruptcy office shall take possession of them and, without further formalities, realise them and distribute the proceeds to the creditors who have suffered a loss in accordance with their ranking.

<sup>2</sup> The bankruptcy office shall proceed in the same way with deposited amounts that are released or have not been withdrawn after ten years.[^269-1]

<sup>3</sup> If the right is dubious, the bankruptcy office shall bring the case to the attention of the bankruptcy creditors by public notice or by post, and the provisions of Article 260 apply *mutatis mutandis*.

[^269-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### C. Deadline for completing bankruptcy proceedings

#### Art. 270

<sup>1</sup> The bankruptcy proceedings should be completed within one year of the declaration of bankruptcy.[^270-1]

<sup>2</sup> This period may be extended by the supervisory authority if necessary.

[^270-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

## Title Eight Attachment

### A. Grounds for attachment

#### Art. 271

<sup>1</sup>

The creditor may request that assets of a debtor that are located in Switzerland be attached for a debt that is due and not secured by a pledge:[^271-1]

- 1\. if the debtor has no fixed domicile;
- 2\. if the debtor, with the intention of evading the fulfilment of his obligations, removes assets, absconds or makes preparations to abscond;
- 3\. if the debtor is travelling through or is a person who visits fairs and markets, for claims which by their nature must be settled immediately;
- 4\.[^271-2] if the debtor does not reside in Switzerland, no other grounds for attachment exist, but the claim has a sufficient connection to Switzerland or is based on an acknowledgement of debt within the meaning of Article 82 paragraph 1;
- 5\.[^271-3] if the creditor holds a provisional or definitive unpaid debt certificate against the debtor;
- 6\.[^271-4] if the creditor has a court judgment or equivalent pursuant to Article 80.

<sup>2</sup> In the cases referred to in numbers 1 and 2, attachment may also be demanded for a debt that is not yet due; doing so causes the claim to become due against the debtor.

<sup>3</sup> In the case referred to in paragraph 1 number 6, the court shall also decide on the enforceability of foreign judgments that are to be enforced in accordance with the Convention of 30 October 2007[^271-5] on Jurisdiction and the Recognition and Enforcement of Judgments in Civil and Commercial Matters.[^271-6]

[^271-1]: Amended by Art. 3 no 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Approval and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).
[^271-2]: Amended by Art. 3 no 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Approval and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).
[^271-3]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^271-4]: Inserted by Art. 3 no 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Approval and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).
[^271-5]: [SR 0.275.12](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/1256)
[^271-6]: Amended by Art. 3 no 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Approval and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).

### B. Authorisation of attachment

#### Art. 272

[^272-1]

<sup>1</sup>

Attachment shall be authorised by the court at the place of debt enforcement or at the place where the assets are located if the creditor can credibly demonstrate that:[^272-2]

- 1\. his claim exists;
- 2\. there is a ground for attachment;
- 3\. there are assets available that belong to the debtor.

<sup>2</sup> If the creditor is domiciled abroad and does not designate a place of service in Switzerland, the place of service is the debt enforcement office.

[^272-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^272-2]: Amended by Art. 3 no 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Approval and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).

### C. Liability for loss or damage due to attachment

#### Art. 273

[^273-1]

<sup>1</sup> The creditor shall be liable both to the debtor and to third parties for any loss or damage arising from an unjustified attachment. The court may require the creditor to provide security.

<sup>2</sup> The action for damages may also be filed in the court at the place of attachment.

[^273-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### D. Attachment order

#### Art. 274

<sup>1</sup> The court shall instruct the debt enforcement officer or another official or employee to execute the attachment and serve the attachment order on him.[^274-1]

<sup>2</sup>

The attachment order shall contain:

- 1\. the name and address of the creditor and his legal representative if applicable and of the debtor;
- 2\. details of the claim in respect of which the attachment is being made;
- 3\. the grounds for the attachment;
- 4\. details of the items to be attached;
- 5\. reference to the creditor’s liability for damages and, if applicable, to the security he is required to provide.

[^274-1]: Amended by Art. 3 no 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Approval and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).

### E. Execution of the attachment

#### Art. 275

[^275-1]

Articles 91–109 on distraint proceedings apply *mutatis mutandis* to the execution of the attachment.

[^275-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### F. Deed of attachment

#### Art. 276

<sup>1</sup> The public official or employee responsible for execution shall draw up the deed of attachment by certifying on the attachment order that the attachment has been executed and listing the attached assets and their estimated value, and shall deliver the same immediately to the debt enforcement office.

<sup>2</sup> The debt enforcement office shall immediately serve the creditor and the debtor with a copy of the deed of attachment and shall notify third parties whose rights are affected by the attachment.[^276-1]

[^276-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### G. Provision of security by the debtor

#### Art. 277

The debtor shall be allowed to freely dispose of the attached assets to the extent that he provides security so that in the event of distraint proceedings or a declaration of bankruptcy the attached assets or other assets of equivalent value will be available. Security shall be provided by deposit, by a joint and several guarantee or by another equivalent form of security.[^277-1]

[^277-1]: Sentence in accordance with No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### H. Opposition to the attachment order

#### Art. 278

[^278-1]

<sup>1</sup> Any person whose rights are affected by an attachment may file opposition in court within ten days of becoming aware of the attachment order.

<sup>2</sup> The court shall give the parties the opportunity to state their case and shall issue an immediate decision.

<sup>3</sup> The decision on opposition may be contested by filing an appeal under the CPC[^278-2]. New circumstances may be pleaded before the appeal court.

<sup>4</sup> Neither opposition nor an appeal compromises the effect of the attachment.

[^278-1]: Amended by Art. 3 No 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Adoption and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).
[^278-2]: [SR 272](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6136)

### I. Validation of attachment

#### Art. 279

[^279-1]

<sup>1</sup> If the creditor has not begun debt enforcement proceedings or filed an action before the attachment is authorised, he must do so within ten days of service of the deed of attachment.

<sup>2</sup> If the debtor files an objection to the summons for payment, the creditor must within ten days of receiving the creditor’s copy of the summons apply to have the objection set aside or file an action for the recognition of his claim. If his application to have the objection set aside is rejected, he must file the action within ten days of receiving notice of the decision[^279-2].[^279-3]

<sup>3</sup> If the debtor has not filed an objection to the summons for payment, the creditor must within 20 days of receiving the creditor’s copy of the summons file a request for continuation of the proceedings. If the objection to the summons is subsequently dismissed, the 20-day period begins once the dismissal of the objection is legally binding. Depending on the debtor’s legal personality, the debt enforcement proceedings shall be continued by way of debt enforcement proceedings by distraint or of debt enforcement proceedings through bankruptcy.[^279-4]

<sup>4</sup> If the creditor has filed a court action in respect of his claim without prior debt enforcement proceedings, he must begin debt enforcement proceedings within ten days of receiving notice of the decision.

<sup>5</sup>

The deadlines in this Article do not run:

- 1\. during the opposition proceedings and in the event of an appeal against the decision on opposition;
- 2\. during proceedings to obtain a declaration of enforceability under the Convention of 30 October 2007[^279-5] on Jurisdiction and the Recognition and Enforcement of Judgments in Civil and Commercial Matters and in the event of an appeal against the decision on the declaration of enforceability.[^279-6]

[^279-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^279-2]: Corrected by the Federal Assembly Drafting Committee (Art. 58 para. 1 ParlA; [SR [171.10](http://www.bk.admin.ch/ch/d/sr/c171_10.html)](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/4991)).
[^279-3]: Amended by Art. 3 No 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Adoption and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).
[^279-4]: Amended by Art. 3 No 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Adoption and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).
[^279-5]: [SR 0.275.12](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/1256)
[^279-6]: Inserted by Art. 3 No 2 of the FedD of 11 Dec. 2009 (Adoption and Implementation of the Lugano Convention), in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 5601](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/800); [BBl 2009 1777](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2009/402)).

### K. Invalidity

#### Art. 280

[^280-1]

The attachment shall cease to have effect if the creditor:

- 1\. fails to comply with the deadlines under Article 279;
- 2\. withdraws the action or the debt enforcement proceedings or allows either to lapse; or
- 3\. loses his action and the court decision becomes binding.

[^280-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### L. Provisional participation in distraint proceedings

#### Art. 281

<sup>1</sup> If, following issue of the attachment order, the attached assets are distrained by another creditor before the attachment creditor may file his own application for distraint, the latter shall automatically participate provisionally in the distraint proceedings.

<sup>2</sup> The creditors may deduct the costs of the attachment from the proceeds of the attached assets.

<sup>3</sup> In addition, the attachment does not establish any preferential right.

## Title Nine Special Provisions on Leases and Usufructuary Leases

#### Art. 282

[^282-1]

[^282-1]: Repealed by No II Art. 3 of the FA of 15 Dec. 1989 on the Amendment of the Swiss Code of Obligations (Lease and Usufructuary Lease), with effect from 1 July 1990 ([AS 1990 802](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1990/802_802_802), Final Provision to Title VIII and VIII<sup>bis</sup>; [BBl 1985 I 1389](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1985/1_1389_1369_1201)).

### Retention directory

#### Art. 283

<sup>1</sup> Landlords and lessors of business premises may call on the assistance of the debt enforcement office to temporarily safeguard their special lien (Arts 268 ff. and 299*c* CO[^283-1]) even if the debt enforcement proceedings have not been initiated.[^283-2]

<sup>2</sup> If there is a risk in any delay, the assistance of the police or the communal authority may be sought.

<sup>3</sup> The debt enforcement office shall draw up a list of the items subject to the lien and set the creditor a deadline for initiating debt enforcement proceedings to realise a pledge.

[^283-1]: [SR 220](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5818)
[^283-2]: Amended by No II Art. 3 of the FA of 15 Dec. 1989 on the Amendment of the Swiss Code of Obligations (Lease and Usufructuary Lease), in force since 1 July 1990 ([AS 1990 802](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1990/802_802_802); [BBl 1985 I 1389](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1985/1_1389_1369_1201), Final Provision to Title VIII and VIII<sup>bis</sup>).

### Return of objects

#### Art. 284

If objects have been removed secretly or by force, they may be returned to the rented or leased premises with the help of the police within ten days of their removal. The rights of third parties acting in good faith are reserved. In the event of any dispute, the court shall decide.[^284-1]

[^284-1]: Version of the third sentence pursuant to Annex 1 no II 17 of the Civil Procedure Code of 19 Dec. 2008, in force since 1 Jan. 2011 ([AS 2010 1739](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2010/262); [BBl 2006 7221](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/914)).

## Title Nine<sup>bis</sup> Special Provisions on Trusts

[^tit9bis-1]

[^tit9bis-1]: Inserted by Art. 3 of the FedD of 20 Dec. 2006 on the Adoption and Implementation of the Hague Convention on the Law applicable to Trusts and on their Recognition, in force since 1 July 2007 ([AS 2007 2849](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2007/374); [BBl 2006 551](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/54)).

### A. Debt enforcement proceedings for debts of trusts

#### Art. 284a

<sup>1</sup> If the assets of a trust as defined in Chapter 9*a* of the Federal Act of 18 December 1987[^284a-1] on Private International Law (PILA) are liable for a debt, debt enforcement proceedings must be directed against a trustee as representative of the trust.

<sup>2</sup> The place of enforcement shall be the seat of the trust in accordance with Article 21 paragraph 3 PILA. If the designated place of administration is not in Switzerland, the trust shall be subject to enforcement at the place where it is administered in fact.

<sup>3</sup> The debt enforcement shall be continued as debt enforcement proceedings through bankruptcy. Bankruptcy is limited to the trust assets.

[^284a-1]: [SR 291](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6186)

### B. Bankruptcy of a trustee

#### Art. 284b

In the event of a trustee’s bankruptcy, the trust assets shall be segregated from the bankruptcy estate after deduction of the trustee’s claims against the trust assets.

## Title Ten Avoidance

[^tit10-1]

[^tit10-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### A. Principles

[^tit10-lvlA-1]

[^tit10-lvlA-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

#### Art. 285

<sup>1</sup> Avoidance actions aim to make assets subject to enforcement proceedings where they have been made unavailable by means of a legal act under Articles 286–288.[^285-1]

<sup>2</sup>

The following persons are entitled to bring an avoidance action:[^285-2]

- 1\.[^285-3] any creditor who has received a provisional or definitive unpaid debt certificate in distraint proceedings;
- 2\. the bankruptcy administration or, in accordance with the Articles 260 and 269 paragraph 3, any individual creditor in bankruptcy.

<sup>3</sup> Avoidance actions may not be brought in the case of legal acts that were carried out during a composition moratorium if they were approved by a composition court[^285-4] or by a creditors’ committee (Art. 295*a*).[^285-5]

<sup>4</sup> Other liabilities entered into during the moratorium with the consent of the commissioner are also not subject to avoidance.[^285-6]

[^285-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^285-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^285-3]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^285-4]: Term pursuant to No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)). This amendment has been made throughout the text.
[^285-5]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).
[^285-6]: Inserted by Annex No 4 of the FA of 19 June 2020 (Company Law), in force since 1 Jan. 2023 ([AS 2020 4005](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/746); [2022 109](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/109); [BBl 2017 399](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2017/112)).

### B. Types

### 1. Avoidance of a gift

#### Art. 286

<sup>1</sup> With the exception of customary occasional gifts, all gifts and gratuitous dispositions made by the debtor within the last year prior to distraint or the declaration of bankruptcy are subject to avoidance.[^286-1]

<sup>2</sup>

The following are treated in the same way as gifts:

- 1\. legal transactions by which the debtor has accepted a consideration that is disproportionate to his own performance;
- 2\.[^286-2] legal transactions through which the debtor has acquired a life annuity, lifetime maintenance, a usufruct or a right of residence for himself or for a third party.

<sup>3</sup> If an action in favour of a close associate or relative of the debtor is contested, the latter bears the burden of proof that there is no imbalance between performance and consideration. Close associates also include companies within a group.[^286-3]

[^286-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^286-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^286-3]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 2. Avoidance for overindebtedness

#### Art. 287

<sup>1</sup>

The following legal acts are subject to avoidance if the debtor performed them within the last year before distraint or the declaration of bankruptcy and was already overindebted at the time they were performed:[^287-1]

- 1\.[^287-2] provision of security for existing liabilities that the debtor was not previously obliged to provide;
- 2\. repayment of a monetary debt by means other than cash or other customary means of payment;
- 3\. payment of a debt that is not yet due.

<sup>2</sup> However, avoidance is excluded if the beneficiary proves that he did not know and should not have known of the debtor’s overindebtedness.[^287-3]

<sup>3</sup>

Avoidance is excluded in particular if securities, intermediated securities or other financial instruments traded on a representative market have been provided as collateral and the debtor has previously:

- 1\. undertaken to increase the collateral in the event of changes in the value of the collateral or in the amount of the secured liability; or
- 2\. been granted the right to replace collateral with collateral of the same value.[^287-4]

[^287-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^287-2]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^287-3]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^287-4]: Inserted by Annex No 4 of the Intermediated Securities Act of 3 Oct. 2008, in force since 1 Jan. 2010 ([AS 2009 3577](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2009/450); [BBl 2006 9315](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2006/1228)).

### 3. Avoidance of wilful unlawful acts

#### Art. 288

[^288-1]

<sup>1</sup> Any legal acts that the debtor carried out within the five years before distraint or the declaration of bankruptcy with the intention, recognisable to the other party, of prejudicing his creditors or favouring individual creditors to the prejudice of the others are ultimately subject to avoidance.

<sup>2</sup> When seeking avoidance of an act in favour of a close associate or relative of the debtor, the associate or relative bears the burden of proving that the intention to cause prejudice was not recognisable. Close associates also include companies in a group of companies.[^288-2]

[^288-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).
[^288-2]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 4. Calculation of the relevant periods

#### Art. 288a

[^288a-1]

The following are not considered when calculating the relevant periods under Articles 286–288:

- 1\. the duration of any preceding composition moratorium;
- 2\. in the case of liquidation of an estate by the bankruptcy office, the time between the date of death and ordering liquidation;
- 3\. the duration of the prior debt enforcement proceedings.

[^288a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)). Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### C. Avoidance action

### 1. Place of jurisdiction

#### Art. 289

[^289-1]

The avoidance action shall be filed in the court at the domicile of the defendant. If the defendant is not domiciled in Switzerland, the action may be filed in the court at the place of the distraint or bankruptcy proceedings.

[^289-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### 2. Respondents

#### Art. 290

[^290-1]

An avoidance action may be brought against persons who have concluded legal transactions subject to avoidance with the debtor or who have been favoured by the debtor in a manner subject to avoidance, as well as against their heirs or other general successors and against third parties acting in bad faith. The rights of third parties acting in good faith are not affected by an avoidance action.

[^290-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### D. Effect

#### Art. 291

<sup>1</sup> Any person who has acquired any of the debtor’s assets through a legal act subject to avoidance is obliged to return them. The consideration must be reimbursed to the extent that it is still in the hands of the debtor or the debtor has been enriched by it. Otherwise, reimbursement may only be claimed as a debt due by the debtor.

<sup>2</sup> If the legal act subject to avoidance consisted in the repayment of a debt, the debt shall be reinstated upon restitution of the amount received.

<sup>3</sup> The bona fide recipient of a gift is only obliged to make restitution up to the amount of his enrichment.

### E. Statute of limitations

#### Art. 292

[^292-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The right to seek avoidance becomes time-barred:

- 1\. on expiry of three years from service of the unpaid debt certificate in distraint proceedings (Art. 285 para. 2 no 1);
- 2\. on expiry of three years from the declaration of bankruptcy (Art. 285 para. 2 no 2);
- 3\. on expiry of three years from confirmation of the composition agreement with assignment of assets.

<sup>2</sup> When recognising a foreign bankruptcy decree, the time between the application for recognition and publication in accordance with Article 169 PILA[^292-2] is not taken into account.

[^292-1]: Amended by no III of the FA of 15 June 2018 (Revision of the Statute of Limitations), in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2018 5343](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2018/807); [BBl 2014 235](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2014/65)).
[^292-2]: [SR 291](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6186)

## Title Eleven Composition Proceedings

[^tit11-1]

[^tit11-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 16 Dec. 1994, in force since 1 Jan. 1997 ([AS 1995 1227](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/1995/1227_1227_1227); [BBl 1991 III 1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/1991/3_1_1_1)).

### I. Composition Moratorium

### A. Initiation

#### Art. 293

[^293-1]

Composition proceedings are initiated by:

- a. an application by the debtor with the following documents attached: an up-to-date balance sheet, a profit and loss account and a liquidity plan or corresponding documents showing the debtor’s current and future assets, earnings or income situation, as well as a provisional restructuring plan;
- b. a request by a creditor who would be entitled to file a bankruptcy petition;
- c. the transfer of files in accordance with Article 173*a* paragraph 2.

[^293-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### B. Provisional moratorium

### 1. Authorisation

#### Art. 293a

[^293a-1]

<sup>1</sup> The composition court shall immediately authorise a provisional moratorium and take any further measures *ex officio* that are necessary to preserve the debtor’s assets. The provisional moratorium may be extended by the composition court on request.

<sup>2</sup> The total duration of the provisional moratorium may not exceed four months. At the request of the commissioner or, if no commissioner has been appointed, of the debtor, the provisional moratorium may be extended by a maximum of four months in justified cases.[^293a-2]

<sup>3</sup> If there is clearly no prospect of restructuring or confirmation of a composition agreement, the composition court shall declare bankruptcy *ex officio*.

[^293a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).
[^293a-2]: Amended by Annex No 4 of the FA of 19 June 2020 (Company Law), in force since 20 Oct. 2020 ([AS 2020 4005](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/746), [4145](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/758); [BBl 2017 399](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2017/112)).

### 2. Provisional commissioner

#### Art. 293b

[^293b-1]

<sup>1</sup> The composition court shall appoint one or more provisional commissioners to further examine the prospect of restructuring or confirmation of a composition agreement. Article 295 applies *mutatis mutandis*.

<sup>2</sup> In justified cases, the appointment of a commissioner may be dispensed with.

[^293b-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 3. Effects of the provisional moratorium

#### Art. 293c

[^293c-1]

<sup>1</sup> The provisional moratorium has the same effect as a definitive moratorium.

<sup>2</sup>

In justified cases, public notice may be dispensed with until the end of the provisional moratorium, provided that the protection of third parties is guaranteed and a corresponding request has been made. In such a case:

- a. the offices are not notified;
- b. debt enforcement proceedings may be initiated against the debtor, but not continued;
- c. the legal consequence of Article 297 paragraph 4 only arises if and when notice of the provisional moratorium is given to the assignee;
- d. a provisional commissioner must be appointed.

[^293c-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 4. Appellate remedies

#### Art. 293d

[^293d-1]

The authorisation of the provisional moratorium and the appointment of the provisional commissioner is not subject to appeal.

[^293d-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### C. Definitive moratorium

### 1. Hearing and decision

#### Art. 294

[^294-1]

<sup>1</sup> If it emerges during the provisional moratorium that there is a prospect of restructuring or confirmation of a composition agreement, the composition court shall grant the moratorium definitively for a further four to six months; it shall decide *ex officio* before the provisional moratorium expires.

<sup>2</sup> The debtor and, if applicable, the requesting creditor must be summoned to a hearing in advance. The provisional commissioner shall report orally or in writing. The court may hear other creditors.

<sup>3</sup> If there is no prospect of restructuring or confirmation of a composition agreement, the court shall declare bankruptcy *ex officio*.

[^294-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 2. Commissioner

#### Art. 295

[^295-1]

<sup>1</sup> The composition court shall appoint one or more commissioners.

<sup>2</sup>

The commissioner shall have the following duties in particular:

- a. he drafts the composition agreement if it is required;
- b. he monitors the acts of the debtor;
- c. he fulfils the duties set out in Articles 298–302 and 304;
- d. he prepares interim reports on the order of the composition court and informs the creditors on the progress of the moratorium.

<sup>3</sup> The composition court may assign the commissioner further duties.

<sup>4</sup> Articles 8, 8*a*, 10, 11, 14, 17-19, 34 and 35 apply *mutatis mutandis* to the commissioner’s management.[^295-2]

[^295-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).
[^295-2]: Inserted by Annex No 4 of the FA of 19 June 2020 (Company Law), in force since 1 Jan. 2023 ([AS 2020 4005](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/746); [2022 109](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/109); [BBl 2017 399](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2017/112)).

### 3. Creditors’ committee

#### Art. 295a

[^295a-1]

<sup>1</sup> Where the circumstances require, the composition court shall appoint a creditors’ committee; the various classes of creditor must be appropriately represented therein.

<sup>2</sup> The creditors’ committee shall supervise the commissioner; it may issue him with recommendations and he must report to it regularly on the progress with the proceedings.

<sup>3</sup> The creditors’ committee shall grant authorisation for transactions under Article 298 paragraph 2 in lieu of the composition court.

[^295a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 4. Extension of the moratorium

#### Art. 295b

[^295b-1]

<sup>1</sup> At the request of the commissioner, the moratorium may be extended to twelve months or, in particularly complex cases, to a maximum of 24 months.

<sup>2</sup> In the event of an extension beyond twelve months, the commissioner must convene a creditors’ meeting, which must be held before the end of the ninth month after the definitive moratorium was granted. Article 301 applies *mutatis mutandis*.

<sup>3</sup> The commissioner shall inform the creditors of the status of the proceedings and the reasons for the extension. The creditors may appoint or dismiss a creditors’ committee and individual members and appoint a new commissioner. Article 302 paragraph 2 applies *mutatis mutandis*.

[^295b-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 5. Appellate remedies

#### Art. 295c

[^295c-1]

<sup>1</sup> The debtor and the creditors may contest the decision of the composition court by filing an appeal under the CPC[^295c-2].

<sup>2</sup> An appeal against the authorisation of the moratorium may not be granted suspensive effect.

[^295c-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).
[^295c-2]: [SR 272](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6136)

### 6. Public notice

#### Art. 296

[^296-1]

The composition court shall give public notice of the authorisation of the moratorium and the debt enforcement office, the commercial register office and the land register office shall be notified immediately. The composition moratorium must be noted in the land register no later than two days after authorisation.

[^296-1]: Amended by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### 7. Termination

#### Art. 296a

[^296a-1]

<sup>1</sup> If restructuring is successful before the moratorium expires, the composition court shall terminate the moratorium *ex officio*. Article 296 applies *mutatis mutandis*.

<sup>2</sup> The debtor and, if applicable, the requesting creditor must be summoned to a hearing. The commissioner shall report orally or in writing. The court may hear other creditors.

<sup>3</sup> The decision to terminate may be contested by filing an appeal under the CPC[^296a-2].

[^296a-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).
[^296a-2]: [SR 272](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6136)

### 8. Declaration of bankruptcy

#### Art. 296b

[^296b-1]

Bankruptcy shall be declared *ex officio* before the moratorium expires if:

- a. this is necessary to preserve the debtor’s assets;
- b. there is obviously no longer any prospect of restructuring or confirmation of a composition agreement; or
- c. the debtor contravenes Article 298 or the instructions of the commissioner.

[^296b-1]: Inserted by No I of the FA of 21 June 2013, in force since 1 Jan. 2014 ([AS 2013 4111](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2013/757); [BBl 2010 6455](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/fga/2010/1144)).

### D. Effects of the moratorium

### 1. On the rights of the creditors

#### Art. 297

[^297-1]

<sup>1</sup> During the moratorium, debt enforcement proceedings may neither be initiated nor continued against the debtor. This does not apply to debt enforcement proceedings to realise a pledge for claims secured by mortgage; the actual realisation, on the other hand, remains excluded.

<sup>2</sup> Article 199 paragraph 2 applies *mutatis mutandis* to distrained assets.

<sup>3</sup> Attachment and other protective measures are excluded for composition claims.

<sup>4</sup> If the assignment of a future claim was agreed before the composition moratorium was authorised, this assignment shall have no effect if the claim only arises after the composition moratorium was authorised.
